‘The Odyssey’ Cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema Breaks Down Filming Climactic Thunderstorm Scene in Imax

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christopher Nolan shot “The Odyssey” to be experienced on the biggest screen possible so audiences could feel the full impact and be fully immersed in that experience — so everything was shot practically.

One of the most challenging sequences to pull off was the film’s final storm scene as Matt Damon’s Odysseus faces the wrath of Poseidon and the full force of Zeus. It was to be climactic and intense, and Odysseus loses all his men as a result of the final insult to the gods. Related Stories Netflix Names Mattel Executive Roberto Stanichi as VP of Consumer Products and Experiences 'Russell Brand: Open Secret' Director Says Finding Contributors Wasn't Easy: 'There's Been a Landscape of Fear'

“We had been talking about going out there to sea, sit on real boats and shoot on Imax,” cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema explains.

A large amount of the film was shot on the open ocean, where Nolan, his cast and crew spent four months capturing how hard the journey would have been as they endured crashing waves. And while the climactic moment ended up being shot in Los Angeles, Nolan didn’t spare any expense in pulling off the sequence, relying instead on old-fashioned filmmaking.Speaking with Variety for Inside the Frame, Van Hoytema explains the scene provided a challenge: “How to replicate something and make it feel visceral and real? How can we do that and avoid a huge amount of CG?”Two decommissioned jet engines were used as wind machines. Van Hoytema’s chief lighting technician and gaffer Adam Chambers built light boxes that would emulate thunder and lightning. The boat was then put on a gimbal, all while being shot on an Imax camera. “It was like shooting a real storm, just under safety supervision,” Van Hoytema says.He adds, “We did a lot of different setups in that whole scene. It is designed to feel extremely chaotic; it’s designed to sort of not understand really what’s going on.”The lighting design was something he also had to think about. “It was interesting because I was thinking, if you’re out at sea at night 2,000 years ago, without electricity and you end up in a thunderstorm, what would that feel like? I was thinking, if you can see everything around you, that’s going to look fake.”

A CGI storm didn’t feel right because the entire crew had been meticulous about keeping everything as grounded and as experiential as possible. Van Hoytema says the team built overhead lighting rigs that were approximately 60×30 feet, hanging on cranes above the ship. “It meant that you would see flashes that progressively move from behind them to the front of them, as it opens up the sky. “What you see is short bursts and there’s just darkness or a little bit of a glow. It’s like trying to see this relentless endless sea, this weather, this saltwater and they’re just so close to death. It’s extremely claustrophobic at the same time.”

When it came to framing Odysseus, Van Hoytema was wired in to keep him in place on the gimbal. His Imax camera on his shoulder was also wired in.

“I was not trying to be in any way precious about framing whatsoever. I was just trying through with a jet engine blowing, gallons of water in my face. I was literally just trying to identify if it was even Matt Damon in the shot. It was extremely visceral, and it’s almost hard to grasp. It was not a situation in which you could just start a take or not. Every take was an event.”

Van Hoytema says the camera starts to roll and the jet engines start. “They rise, and at some point, they sound like two fighter jets are next to your ear. And it gets really wild because they start moving the gimbal as well, and you’re holding on for your life. The camera gets turned; it’s the original Imax camera, not the blimp and the Imax camera is very loud. However, in that situation, you don’t even know if it’s turned on. You do a take, and you have a crazy powerful adrenaline rush.”

In contrast, Van Hoytema recalled a moment when the crew was out to sea, in open waters and a pod of bottlenose dolphins appeared. It was completely unplanned.

Van Hoytema recalls, “It’s interesting because it was also pretty violent that day. It was very harsh weather and I remember a lot of people on the crew got seasick. As we started filming, dolphins started to jump out of the water. What is interesting is that it symbolizes that no matter what the weather does, we take the weather as it comes and accept it as the reality you work in that day.”

Shondaland Taps Amy Joy Rivkin and Karyn Smith-Forge for Key Executive Roles

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Rivkin will be vp production at the company, and Smith-Forge will serve as vp scripted series.

Shondaland is adding to its executive ranks.

The production company founded by Shonda Rhimes and Betsy Beers has named Amy Joy Rivkin as vp production and Karyn Smith-Forge as vp scripted series. Rivkin comes to Shondaland from Media Res, and Smith-Forge from Don Cheadle’s production company This Radicle Act.

“Amy Joy’s impressive track record steering complex, premium productions and Karyn’s vast experience and sharp instincts across scripted genres make them powerful additions to Shondaland,” said Alison Eakle, Shondaland’s chief content officer. “We’re thrilled we get to shape our next chapter of storytelling alongside them.” Related Stories TV Shonda Rhimes Signs New Overall Deal at Netflix TV Kim Raver on Leaving 'Grey's Anatomy': "Who Gets to Play a Character for This Long?"

Rivkin will oversee physical production on Shondaland’s shows, which include Netflix’s Bridgerton, ABC’s Grey’s Anatomy and its upcoming spinoff and a number of projects in development. She spent the past four years at The Morning Show and Pachinko producer Media Res. Prior to that, she was at Netflix, handling production management for the streamer’s original drama series, and Legendary Entertainment.

As vp scripted series, Smith-Forge will lead development of new series from Shondaland at Netflix, where the company has a rich overall deal. Smith-Forge was most recently president of This Radicle Act, working with Cheadle on TV and film projects at a number of outlets, including Netflix, Showtime, AMC, FX and HBO Max.

She was previously senior vp production at Berlanti Productions, where she managed a slate of some 20 series, including The CW’s Arrow-verse.

James Bond Fans React To Robert Pattinson's 007 Casting

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson has spent his career ping-ponging between major franchise fare ("Harry Potter," "Twilight," "The Batman") and weird arthouse fare ("The Lighthouse," "Good Time," "Cosmopolis"). Along the way, he's garnered a pretty broad fanbase, so there are plenty of people celebrating the news that Pattinson will be taking over the role of James Bond.

There were several other finalists to play the next Bond, including Josh O'Connor, Jack Lowden, and Nicholas Hoult, but Pattinson was the clear frontrunner even before he was announced. Early reactions to the news have noted the somewhat absurd flex of landing the role of James Bond just a few years after landing the role of Batman. "This is the greed they talk about in the Bible," wrote one user on Reddit.

Casting such a well-known actor in the role of Bond bucks the trend of 007 casting, with previous revamps traditionally favoring actors who weren't already firmly established. Many people, while not necessarily disliking the choice of Pattinson for the role, have pointed out that Amazon isn't exactly taking a risk. Other Reddit users describe Pattinson as "a very conservative choice" and "very safe casting," with one wryly commenting, "This isn't beating the allegations of 'every movie has the same five actors in it.'" So, while casting a lesser-known actor would surely have attracted some controversy in its own right, some Bond fans feel that the safety of this choice is "disappointing."

And, of course, there are Batman fans who fear that this fresh commitment spells the end of Pattinson's tenure as the Dark Knight. Between production delays on "The Batman II," a recent power shake-up at DC Studios, and now this 007 casting news, the general assumption is that Pattinson's Batman will be hanging up his cape after two movies.

One of the upsides of having such an eccentric career and personality is that Robert Pattinson has generated a great many memes over the years — and now they're being put to good use. One Tumblr user adapted a famous "Twilight" scene to have Pattinson's Edward Cullen saying, "This is the skin of a [license to kill]er," Bella." Others, meanwhile, have joked that we "finally have a Gen Z James Bond," in reference to a Vanity Fair interview wherein Pattinson doggedly insists that he is Gen Z and that the year he was born "doesn't matter."

One meme that became popular even before Pattinson was officially cast imagines the reactions of the other hopefuls auditioning for the role when they see the unstoppable Pattinson walk in after them.

every other actor when robert pattinson shows up to audition pic.twitter.com/40fHsnS2C4

— ivy (@ohhhhhherewegoo) November 16, 2026

Fans are particularly amused by the fact that Pattinson showed up "late" to the audition process (which has been going on for years), sandwiching it in between his commitments to "Dune: Part Three" and "The Batman Part II," and still landed the role. But that kind of effortless swagger only makes him more perfect for the role of James Bond.

Vikings won't be leaving Minnesota for altitude acclimation

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

With quarterback Kirk Cousins back and the Minnesota defense playing at a championship level, the sky is the limit for the Vikings.

On Sunday, the extent to which they’ll be literally in the sky could limit the ability to get an eighth win.

The Week 11 prime-time showdown between the 7-2 Vikings and 8-1 49ers will happen in Mexico City, where the altitude is 7,280 feet above sea level. The Vikings won’t be relocating this week to acclimate to the thinner air.

The 49ers, in contrast, have gone from Dallas to Colorado, where they’ll train in the far lower distance of 5,280 feet above sea level.

The Vikings will have a plan to get their players ready to perform on an elevated field. Details will emerge this week.

Whatever the plan is, it won’t entail the same kind of direct acclimation in which the 49ers are engaging.

It sets up another apples-to-apples comparison regarding the plans for international games. In Week 1, the 49ers went to Australia early. The Rams went late. And the 49ers dominated L.A., winning 27-7.

This time around, the stakes are extremely high. The winner takes over the de facto driver’s seat for the top seed in the NFC.

A loss means more to the Vikings than the 49ers, as it relates to their respective divisions. San Francisco has a solid cushion over the Seahawks and Rams in the NFC West. The Vikings are caught in a three-way tie for first place in the NFC North.

Dave Franco & Willa Fitzgerald To Lead Lionsgate Horror Film ‘Mommy’s Home’

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Over the summer, Universal unleased one menacing mommy upon theaters — and now, Lionsgate is preparing to unleash another. Dave Franco and Willa Fitzgerald have signed on to star in Mommy’s Home, the studio’s new psychological horror film to be directed by David F. Sandberg.

Details as to the duo’s roles are being kept under wraps. Sandberg, the director of Annabelle: Creation and Lights Out, helms the story of a young dad whose family life becomes terrifyingly unhinged when his cryogenically preserved mom is unfrozen and sent to live with him and his family. Related Stories Casting Aaron Pierre And 'Sisu' Director Jalmari Helander Team on Action Thriller 'Fairytale In New York' From Netflix And Lionsgate Acquisitions Lionsgate Acquires Alma Har'el's 'Wet' Starring Connor Storrie, Rebecca Ferguson, Javier Bardem, Odessa A'zion, Rosalía & Lukas Haas

The project marks a reteam for Lionsgate and Franco following his starring role in the studio’s hit threequel Now You See Me: Now You Don’t, which grossed over $245 million worldwide last fall. Franco and Fitzgerald reteam after co-starring in Paramount’s 2025 Colleen Hoover adaptation Regretting You. Watch on Deadline

The script for Mommy’s Home is by James Morosini, who was attached to direct when we announced the project back in 2024. Producers include Craig Flores, Dawn Olmstead and Anonymous Content, along with Sandberg and Lotta Losten via Mångata. Nathan Kahane and Morosini will exec produce, as will Franco, joined by David Levine and Garrett Kemble on behalf of Anonymous Content.

Scott O’Brien and Maria Ascanio are overseeing the project for Lionsgate. Dan Freedman negotiated the deal on behalf of the studio, which launched international sales out of this year’s AFM.

Lionsgate Motion Picture Group President Erin Westerman told us, “Every once in a while, a script comes along that is so wildly original and impossible to forget that you just have to make it. Mommy’s Home is one of those movies. David F. Sandberg is an exceptional horror filmmaker, and we can’t imagine anyone better suited to bring this particular brand of madness to life.”

Westerman added, “Dave Franco is one of those rare actors who is endlessly watchable and impossible not to root for, even when the world around him is going completely off the rails. He’s been part of the Lionsgate family through Now You See Me, and we couldn’t be happier to be working with him again. And Willa Fitzgerald is going to be a revelation in this film. This is a project that everyone involved is making out of sheer passion, and we can’t wait for audiences to experience just how far it goes.”

Coming off the hard-R Sundance comedy Idiots and DreamWorks’ Forgotten Island, Franco will soon be seen reprising as himself in Season 2 of The Studio. This follows his Emmy nomination for a scene-stealing turn as himself in the CinemaCon-set Season 1 finale. He is repped by WME, Anonymous Content and Toczek Suddleson.

Breaking out with her turn in JT Mollner’s lauded psychological thriller Strange Darling, Fitzgerald has also been seen starring in Season 1 of the hit Amazon series Reacher, as well as Kathryn Bigelow’s Netflix film A House of Dynamite and Regretting You. Upcoming, she’ll be seen in the espionage thriller Billion Dollar Spy, as well as the Sony horror film Skeletons from Strange Darling‘s Mollner and producer J.J. Abrams. She is repped by CAA, Untitled and Jackoway Austen Tyerman.

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Rihanna put rumors of a feud with EJ Johnson to bed over a month after the latter referenced her tardiness to a Dior show at Paris Fashion Week.

Rihanna always knows how to kiss it better.

After EJ Johnson raised eyebrows with a video detailing Rihanna’s tardiness to a Paris Fashion Week show—and when her apparent clap back raised eyebrows even higher—the “Umbrella” singer took a moment to confirm there’s no lingering bad blood.

“There’s no beef, you know there’s no beef,” she said in a video shared by TMZ. “I just felt bad for him, you know?”

In fact, she’s willing to put her best foot forward with EJ—or rather, allow them to.

When asked whether she’d ever let EJ walk in a Savage x Fenty show, the 38-year-old affirmed, “He deserves to.”

6 Superhero Movies From the 2000s That Are 10/10 Masterpieces

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 2000s were huge years for superhero cinema—arguably the biggest for the landscape of all time. It was in this decade that superhero movies, as audiences know them today, were defined, and the standards were set. The years 2000 to 2009 are some of the most significant years and helped birth some of the most popular franchises in cinematic history as a whole.

Sam Raimi's Spider-Man series, the X-Men franchise, the Marvel Cinematic Universe, Christopher Nolan's The Dark Knight series and so much more all got their big breaks in this era. Without these abundantly important 10 years, superhero movies would not even be close to where they are today. Their significance wouldn't even be close to what they are, and events like Avengers: Endgame most likely would not have ever happened or had the success they did without these foundational projects that are, to simply state, masterpieces.

Spider-Man is the most profitable superhero on the planet—he has been for quite some time now—and that meant that no one was surprised that, when the technology was finally there, he'd get a live-action movie. Come 2002, fans were finally treated to Sam Raimi's Spider-Man, which seriously rocked the world. Sure, X-Men came out just a few years earlier, but Spider-Man really helped drive home and fully prove that comic book characters could work on the big screen and the Hollywood landscape.

This 2002 flick was an all-time smash hit and proved that superhero movies could be genuinely amazing financial investments for big studios, with the project grossing an astounding about $825 million worldwide, with a phenomenal $114.8 million opening weekend. It was the first film to ever cross $100 million in its opening weekend and if that's not proof of how great this movie is, then what could be? The performances across the board from especially the likes of Willem Dafoe are wonderful, the directing from Raimi shows how unashamed he was in the source material, and the CGI work was revolutionary for the times.

The first Blade movie was a big moment for comic book movies, being one of the first ones to ever be rated-R and be a success. So, when it came time for the sequel, Blade II, the stakes were high. Thankfully, legendary director Guillermo del Toro and leading man Wesley Snipes, delivered a project that was a great success. It turned the franchise from what the franchise was in Blade to a high-speed, thrilling, gothic, and all-around fire flick.

Guillermo del Toro continues to be a masterclass of a director here with his emphasis on practical effects, making the more horror-based elements of the project sell better than they ever could in CGI. Snipes brings his everything to the role—as per usual—and keeps Blade feeling like a character that the audience genuinely wants to keep watching.

The first X-Men movie was incredibly important. It established these amazing characters and began to send a great message about the effects of discrimination and bigotry. Mutants became the talk of the town, and Hugh Jackman stepped into the boots of Wolverine for the very first time. However, it's highly agreed upon that the sequel, X2: X-Men United, is the superior film for a plethora of reasons.

This is, without a doubt, the Star Wars: Episode V — The Empire Strikes Back for the X-Men franchise. The stakes are raised significantly, and the themes and messaging are more prominent than ever before. It has one of the best opening scenes in a superhero movie—Nightcrawler (Alan Cumming) infiltrating the White House to attack the President (Cotter Smith), brings more classic X-Men characters to the forefront, has a great villain in William Stryker (Brian Cox), and so much more.

2008 was arguably the biggest year in superhero cinema for quite some time because of two major movies: The Dark Knight and none other than the beginning of the MCU itself, Iron Man. Brought to life by the amazing Jon Favreau in the director's chair and Robert Downey Jr. as the Armored Avenger, this 2008 project birthed one of the most popular and profitable cinematic franchises ever put to screen—one that still runs in massive success to this very day.

This served not only as a major comeback for Downey Jr.'s career, but was a huge step in CGI for filmmaking, as well. Iron Man looks perfect in pretty much every shot, and is still looked back on to have better-looking CGI than some modern Marvel films. The biggest standout, however, is the performance from Downey Jr. He solidifies himself at Tony Stark and continued to play him for over 10 years after the release of Iron Man, now even returning to the MCU again in 2026—albeit as a new character, Doctor Doom. This movie is the majority reason the superhero movie landscape has become what it is today.

Four people, four completely different ideas of what it means to have somebody in your corner. Here is who picked up, what they would ask of you, and what you would get back.

You want the friend who does not leave. Steve is the one who turns up at the door without being asked, who remembers what you said you were worried about, and who has never once made you audition for his attention.

You want the friend who is genuinely exhausting and completely worth it. Tony will forget your birthday, then build you something at four in the morning that you did not know you needed, and hand it over as though it were nothing.

You want the friend who replies in four seconds and apologises for it. Peter is earnest in a way that is almost unfashionable, and he will take on far more than he can carry rather than admit that he is struggling.

You want the friend who insults your shoes, fixes your phone and then quietly rearranges your entire life for the better. Shuri does not do soft reassurance, and that is the reason you would believe her when she says you are fine.

The other half of the two major super-films from 2008 is the unforgettable The Dark Knight by super-director Christopher Nolan. This project took a far more gritty and grounded take on the character of Batman (Christian Bale), and set a new style for superhero movies. Even the Spider-Man franchise took a note from this movie's book, with The Amazing Spider-Man also taking a grounded take—which everyone believes is because of the success of The Dark Knight.

Much like Iron Man, The Dark Knight's biggest highlight is within a masterful performance . . . that of Heath Ledger as the Joker. This performance has gone on to be one of the most impactful, defining, and memorable performance in cinematic history as a whole. The Dark Knight is genuinely one of the most impactful superhero movies ever made.

There's no question that when it comes to superhero films that are masterpieces, Spider-Man 2 must enter the conversation. Raimi's big sequel is genuinely known as one of the best movie sequels period. It takes everything that was good about Spider-Man, enhances those things, and adds to them as well. This feels like a borderline perfect superhero movie, and is still considered one to this day. It's so beloved and astounding that it's fair to say it won't be forgotten for quite some time.

With great acting from almost every cast member, iconic action (who could forget the train scene?), great visuals (both in cinematography and visual effects), a heartfelt-yet-heartbreaking screenplay, and a once-again mesmerizing score from Danny Elfman, Spider-Man 2 deserves all the praise it's gotten over the decades. What's so interesting and well-done about Spider-Man 2 is the fact that before anything, it's a Peter Parker (Tobey Maguire) movie—not a Spider-Man movie. The heart and soul of the story lies within Peter and his struggle between his two identities, and it isn't solely fueled by the external conflict—which is more of what Spider-Man did a few years prior. This allows the second entry in this franchise to feel more like a character study before anything else.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Robert Pattinson Is The New James Bond

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It looks like Robert Pattinson is trading in his bat mobile for an Aston Martin. After a highly scrutinized and lengthy audition process involving some of Hollywood's most sought after leading men, it was the Batman star who emerged at the eleventh hour to nab the highly sought after role of M16 agent James Bond.

“James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime,” said Pattinson in a statement. “I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Created by Ian Fleming for his 1953 novel Casino Royale, James Bond, the British intelligence officer who lends his immense talents to M16, is one of the most recognizable characters in the modern world. Figuring prominently in twelve of Fleming’s novels and two short stories, Bond is both tall, dark, and handsome and calm, cool, and collected—the platonic ideal of a world class spy. Bond leapt from page to screen in 1962 with the Eon-produced Dr. No, spawning one of the biggest media franchises in history. Since its inception, the twenty-five Bond films have grossed over $7 billion dollars world wide.

Pattinson follows in the suave footsteps of Daniel Craig, who played James Bond for five films, from 2006's Casino Royale until 2022's No Time To Die where (spoiler alert) Craig's 007 dies while protecting the world from a global terrorist organization. Around No Time To Die, there was another shake up in Bond ownership when Amazon acquired Bond's parent studio, MGM, in an $8.5 billion. Barbara Broccoli, daughter of Eon founder Albert R. “Cubby” Broccoli, producer of the first Bond film and her half brother, Michael G. Broccoli, Barbara held the rights for the franchise for almost 30 years, and pledged to work with Amazon after founder Jeff Bezos. However, in February 2025, the Broccolis announced that they’d be stepping back from the franchise and ceding complete creative control to Amazon MGM. At the Oscars a month later, the Broccoli family was honored with a bizarre musical tribute to James Bond, starring Margaret Qualley, Doja Cat, and Lisa, unofficially ushering in a new era in the Bond Cinematic Universe.

Although a new era of Bond loomed, Amazon MGM had one major problem: they needed a new Bond. Ahead of No Time To Die, Craig had announced that he was stepping away from the franchise, leaving the producers with a 007 sized hole to fill. Before she stepped away, Broccoli insinuated that this new era of Bond was looking for a younger actor, in their 30s, to take over the reins from Craig, who was 51 when he retired from M16.

Technically, they landed on someone slightly outside their preferred age range; Pattinson is 40 years old. Just don't tell him that. During Vanity Fair's lie detector test with Jennifer Lawrence while promoting their film Die My Love, Pattinson insisted he was from a different generation. I am Gen Z," he said to Lawrence. "I am." Hey, you're only as old as you feel. In the same video, Pattinson said he “loves taking jobs of younger actors," which he managed to do with his Bond casting. Good for Pattinson.

Almost every leading man in Hollywood's name came up as a potential predecessor to Craig—except for Pattinson, who the masses assumed was out of contention due to the fact that he locked into playing another beloved fictional character with massive IP: Batman. Pattinson's take on the caped crusader in Matt Reeve's The Batman (2022) was a major success, but several production delays on The Batman Part II, and a shakeup at Batman's studio DC—last week, it was announced that co-CEO's of DC James Gunn and Peter Safran were ousted from the studio—meant that there might be time in Pattinson's schedule for some sexy espionage.

Still, it was far from a done deal that Pattinson would land 007. In recent years, some fans have advocated for a twist on the classic Bond formula. Lashawna Lynch made history as Nomi, the first Black person or woman to ever to hold the 007 moniker in No Time To Die. But Nomi is not James Bond. There was a seemingly endless period when the world was convinced that Idris Elba—arguably the most tall, dark, and handsome man to ever exist—would take on the mantle at some point, but while Elba auditioned, it wasn’t meant to be. Now, at 53, Elba has seemingly aged out of the role. (For the record, Elba thought the next Bond should be a woman.) Elba wasn’t the only name bandied about in recent years. It was reported that Aaron Taylor Johnson was close to signing a contract to take over the role in 2023, but those reports turned out to be false.

Although a favorite at the beginning, Jacob Elordi reportedly didn't screen test for the role. Riz Ahmed made an Emmy-nominated television show, Bait, inspired by the idea of a British Pakastani actor getting cast as Bond. Other actors rumored to be in the mix over the course of the process include, but are not limited to Tom Francis, Harris Dickinson and apparently Harry Styles. At the end of the day five actors screen tested for the role: Slow Horse's Jack Lowden, One Night Only's Callum Turner, Disclosure Day's Josh O'Connor, Superman's Nicholas Hoult, and Pattison. Hoult and Pattinson were the last two to screen test for the role, implying that the powers that be at Amazon MGM may not have been happy with the three options presented and sent a hail mary. When Puck reported that Pattinson was able to negotiate a 3 picture deal rather than a 4 picture deal despite the fact that he commanded a much higher salary then the other options, it looked like the writing was on the wall that Pattinson would be the next Bond.

It also helps that Pattinson is having a career high at the moment, having starred in a handful of the most critically acclaimed and commercially successful films of 2026. He began the year by turning his controversial A24 comedy The Drama with Zendaya into a box office success, generating $134 million worldwide on a budget of $28 million. He then followed that up with a deliciously sniveling turn in Christopher Nolan's epic The Odyssey, an Oscar front-runner which earned $1.8 billion at the box office. Then there's Primetime, Lance Oppenheim's To Catch A Predator drama which stars Pattinson as host Chris Hansen. (Yes, in a way Chris Hansen is now one degree from being James Bond. Let that sink in). Primetime hit big with $61 million globally on an $18 million budget, and is earning Pattinson leading actor buzz. On top of it all, he's still got Dune: Part 3 with Villeneuve coming down the pipeline in December.

Ultimately, the Twilight star was handpicked by the powers that be at Amazon MGM, producers Amy Pascal, David Heyman, executive producer Tanya Lapointe, and director Denis Villeneuve, who is helming the next iteration of the series based on a script from screenwriter Steven Knight. Given the fact that Pattinson is starring in Villeneuve's highly-anticipated Dune: Part 3 it might not be that much of a surprise that the director chose Pattinson as his next Bond. “Before anything else, I want to express my deep gratitude to the remarkable actors who joined us on this journey," said Villeneuve in a statement. "We were surrounded by extraordinary talent, but there can only be one 007. I am overjoyed to welcome Robert Pattinson as our new James Bond. He has the timeless soul of 007 and brings a spirit that is completely new. I cannot wait to begin.”

Pattinson enters a long lineage of spectacularly suave spies—some who lasted longer than others in Bond’s impeccably tailored suits. The first ever James Bond was, of course, Sean Connery, who went on to star in seven Bond films, including From Russia with Love (1963), Goldfinger (1964), and Diamonds Are Forever (1971). Connery was followed by George Lazenby, who starred in a single Bond film, On Her Majesty’s Secret Service (1969), before passing the reins back to Connery. The third Bond was Roger Moore, who took over for 1973’s Live and Let Die, and appeared in a total of seven Bond films, most notably The Spy Who Loved Me (1977) and Octopussy (1983).

After Moore came Timothy Dalton, who became 007 with 1987’s The Living Daylights, then threw in the towel after 1989’s License to Kill. Millennials will fondly remember the next Bond: Pierce Brosnan, who starred as the sexy spy in four films from 1995’s Golden Eye to 2002’s Die Another Day, until Craig took over in 2006.

Will Pattison's Bond be more debonair like Brosnan, who told VF he was “eagerly” awaiting the Bond casting news, or brooding like Craig? Will he be remembered as a rightful heir to Connery's throne, or will his Bond feel closer to Austin Powers in The Spy who Shagged Me? In any case, lets hope Pattinson prefers his martinis shaken not stirred. He's going to be drinking a lot of them for the foreseeable future.

Harrison Ford Exec-Produced Voyaging Film ‘The Navigator’ Unveils Clip Ahead of DOC NYC Premiere (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

“The Navigator,” a voyaging film directed by Jordan Manley and executive produced by Harrison Ford, has unveiled an exclusive clip ahead of its Nov. 19 East Coast bow at DOC NYC.

Ford also supplied a message with the clip.

“‘The Navigator’ is a deeply moving film – one I’m truly grateful for. Its powerful storytelling and cinematic beauty left me in awe. Master navigator Nainoa Thompson, his teachers, and his students are extraordinary examples of profound humility. They help us understand why ancient wisdom matters in a modern world, and remind us what we are capable of when we act in harmony with nature,” Ford said. Related Stories How Netflix‘s Animated 'Steps’ Reimagines the Cinderella Story Through the Eyes of Her Asian Stepsisters and Finds a New Kind of Happily Ever After ‘Virgin River’ Renewed for Season 9 to Become Netflix’s Longest-Running Original Scripted Series

Ford is one of seven executive producers on the project, a group that also includes Yvon Chouinard and Laurene Powell Jobs. Kathy Thompson and Laura Yale are the producers, and Yo-Yo Ma contributes cello to a score by Matthew Robert Cooper.

The film’s world premiere took place Oct. 21 at Hawaiʻi Theater on Oʻahu during the Hawaii International Film Festival, and it left with the Pacific Awards – Feature prize. Thompson, meanwhile, received the Halekulani Legacy Award.

In 1980, Thompson crossed from Hawai’i to Tahiti by voyaging canoe using neither a compass nor modern instruments, becoming the first Native Hawaiian to do so in six centuries.

Now nearly 50 years later, he is making the crossing again, for what the film presents as the last time. Thompson sails with a handful of student navigators. They rely on the stars, natural cues and the lessons of the late Mau Piailug, a master navigator, along a 2,400-mile route their ancestors sailed. Piailug, who was raised on Satawal in Micronesia, taught Native Hawaiians traditional navigation.

Manley said in a director’s statement: “I came to see a deep burden and ‘kuleana’ that rests upon the shoulders of Nainoa Thompson, who has spent the last 50 years of his life fulfilling a promise made to his teacher, Micronesian master navigator Mau Piailug, to prevent navigation from going extinct.”

The director, based in Vancouver, previously made “Treeline” and the series “A Skier’s Journey.” Submarine is handling sales and distribution inquiries.

Paul Rudd And Seann William Scott's R-Rated Comedy Is Worth Revisiting 18 Years Later

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Studios abandoned big comedies years ago. But in the 2000s and early 2010s, we really had it good. This was the age of the Judd Apatow comedy, in which the director not only delivered such modern classics as "The 40-Year-Old Virgin" and "Knocked Up," but influenced almost every comedy released during the era. Alongside the main Apatow offerings you had those which he produced such as "Anchorman: The Legend of Ron Burgundy," or those made by his collaborators, such as "Superbad." Then, there were those films that featured Apatow associates but were made without his direct involvement. The brilliant "I Love You, Man" is one example which, despite being criminally overlooked, remains one of Paul Rudd's best films.

As does 2008's "Role Models," which saw Rudd team up with Seann William Scott for a film that, like pretty much every comedy movie released at the time, was defined by the improvisational, R-rated humor that had made Apatow's films so successful. This particular movie, however, paired Apatow allies Rudd and Christopher Mintz-Plasse with former members of sketch comedy troupe The State in director David Wain and writer/actor Ken Marino.

As someone who watched "Role Models" when it came out, I simply cannot believe this movie is 18 years old. Despite being less well known than the aforementioned "Knocked Up" and its ilk, "Role Models" became instantly beloved by me and my friends upon its release. Almost two decades later, it's strange to think that an entire generation has grown up without films such as this shaping their sense of humor. If you happen to be part of that group, now's the time to acquaint yourself with this  oft-overlooked comedy. If, like me, you remember "Role Models" fondly, you'll be happy to hear it holds up.

Like so many of the best comedies of the 2000s, "Role Models" succeeds because despite being an R-rated comedy with frequent gross-out gags and unapologetically immature humor, it's also a surprisingly tender story with its heart in the right place.

Paul Rudd plays Danny who, alongside Seann William Scott's Anson Wheeler, works as a promoter for the energy drink "Minotaur." Danny feels deeply unfulfilled and has become deeply cynical as a result, spending most of his time correcting people on their speech. This causes his girlfriend, Beth (Elizabeth Banks), to break up with him, after which Danny loses it during a presentation at a high school. His freak out leads to both he and Anson being assigned to community service at Sturdy Wings, a big brother-style program where the pair become mentors to two "littles."

Anson is paired with Ronnie (Bobb'e J. Thompson) a foul-mouthed kid with no friends, while Danny is given Augie Farks (Christopher Mintz-Plasse), a teenager obsessed with his medieval LARP group. At first, Danny and Anson are merely going through the motions. But over time they develop a bond with their "littles" — one which just might melt Danny's cold, cynical heart.

The secret star of "Role Models" is Joe Lo Truglio, who plays Kuzzik, a particularly dedicated LARP-er who, if you watch the bloopers, made it almost impossible for Rudd to get through any of his scenes without breaking. But there are a ton of standout performances here, from A.D. Miles as overly earnest Sturdy Wings counsellor Martin Gary to Ken Jeong as the self-serious LARP king Argotron and Jane Lynch as a former addict turned Sturdy Wings leader.

"Role Models" hit theaters in November 2008 and grossed a solid $92.6 million on a $28 million budget. It also garnered positive reviews, earning a 77% "certified fresh" rating on Rotten Tomatoes. Dana Stevens of Slate found "Role Models" to be "strewn with enough serendipities to make it a worthy variation on the dudes-grow-up genre," while the San Francisco Chronicle's Peter Hartlaub noted how, while Seann William Scott and Paul Rudd were "from two different moviemaking cliques" they ultimately "get along fine in a Bing Crosby/David Bowie kind of way."

Meanwhile, Rafer Guzman of Newsday noted how Rudd, who co-wrote the script with David Wain, Timothy Dowling, and Ken Marino, had "clearly learned a few things working on Judd Apatow comedies." By that point, Rudd had appeared in Apatow's "The 40-Year-Old Virgin" and "Knocked Up," as well as several Apatow-produced projects including "Anchorman" and "Forgetting Sarah Marshall." As such, he was well-established as one of the most prominent figures of the 2000s R-rated comedy boom.

But The State boys certainly brought their own flair to the film and the pairing of theirs and Rudd's sensibility along with Scott's lovable knucklehead charm made for a hilarious and surprisingly heartwarming experience that deserves to be remembered as fondly as some of the more well-known comedies of the era. If you fancy giving one of the most underrated buddy comedies of the 2000s another go, or are yet to see the "Role Models," the movie is available to watch for free on Pluto TV.

Sean McVay: Aaron Donald never ceases to amaze me, he's one-of-one

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For the first time in 1,050 days, Rams defensive tackle Aaron Donald recorded a sack when he brought down rookie Carson Beck during the second quarter of Sunday’s victory over the Cardinals.

It was the first of two takedowns he was involved in on the day, as he was also credited for a half-sack later in the afternoon.

After the contest, head coach Sean McVay told reporters Donald “never ceases to amaze me.”

“I’m never surprised,” McVay said in his press conference. “I think the term ‘one-of-one’ is thrown around way too loosely in this day and age, but it’s not with him. He’s one-of-one. He can do things, he’s done things and his impact — whether he has to say it or just show it, he’s a total ‘G.’ I love everything he’s about, and he is one-of-one.

“He’s had a tremendous impact on this team and it certainly is not exclusive to just getting a sack today. He’s made a tremendous impact. He’s a guy that everybody looks at and they say, ‘Watching the way that he moves — if you just mimic and emulate that, we’ll be in good shape.’”

Donald has tallied 15 total tackles with four tackles for loss, four QB hits, and now 1.5 sacks since returning from his retirement at the start of the season.

Robert Pattinson's Batman Movie Franchise Is Officially Finished

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No, I didn’t forget about The Dark Knight Rises

With Robert Pattinson confirmed to be the next James Bond, one of the biggest takeaways is that Matt Reeves’ The Batman series will likely end with The Batman: Part II. Pattinson is signed on for three films as Bond, and while that’s one less film than the four-film deal that Amazon MGM Studios was seeking for the other Bond contenders, it’s pretty hard to imagine that Pattinson will juggle the role of 007 with an ongoing commitment to DC — much less Battinson’s integration into the wider DCU, which some DC fans had hoped for. To be clear, nothing has been confirmed about the status of a third The Batman movie, but the latest news combined with the extreme delays facing The Batman Part II feel like a pretty clear indication that the Batmobile has hit a dead end and will need yet another reboot.

However, the loss is even bigger than you might realize, as this will be the third time in a row that Batman fans will be robbed of a good Batman trilogy.

Tim Burton’s 1989 film Batman, of course, revolutionized the character of Batman and comic book movies as a whole. Before then, the general public’s awareness of Batman was mostly associated with the campy, comedic Adam West Batman, but Burton changed that and presented a dark, heavily stylized version starring Michael Keaton. He then followed it up with Batman Returns, which many still consider to be one of the best Batman films ever.

Warner Bros., however, felt it was too dark and got rid of Burton for the third film, replacing him with Joel Schumacher. Keaton followed Burton out the door, not wanting to continue as Batman without him. Batman Forever, starring Val Kilmer, was the result, and while that film has its defenders, it felt like a different franchise and a different Batman altogether. The same can be said for Batman and Robin.

Then in 2005 came Batman Begins, directed by Christopher Nolan and starring Christian Bale. Taking a far more grounded approach to the character, Batman Begins turned Batman’s origin into an epic film saga, where it had only been relegated to brief flashbacks in previous films. Nolan topped himself with The Dark Knight by putting Batman in the center of a gritty crime drama with Heath Ledger’s Oscar-winning turn as the Joker. For a second time, a Batman film revolutionized comic book films. Nearly 20 years later, many still cite The Dark Knight as the greatest comic book movie ever made.

Unfortunately, Nolan’s third Batman film wasn’t nearly as good as the first two. Perhaps because of Ledger’s untimely death before the release of The Dark Knight, Nolan at first seemed disinterested in a third film, and it is generally understood that he had to rework the story he had in mind to remove the Joker from its narrative. Regardless of the reasons why, The Dark Knight Rises is a deeply uneven, disorganized film that most view as the low point of Nolan’s Batman, if not an outright bad film (I tend to think it is quite bad).

After Nolan’s trilogy concluded, Ben Affleck became the next Batman, in both Batman v. Superman: Dawn of Justice as well as the various versions of Justice League. Regardless of what you think of these films — I personally find them insufferable — they were deeply divisive. Despite an Affleck-led Batman film being rumored for a time, we never got him in any standalone films, much less three of them.

In 2022, The Batman offered new hope. While some were skeptical of Pattinson as Batman, he proved to be a really solid take on the Dark Knight, which prioritized Batman’s skills as a detective, something missing from previous Batman films. He was complemented by Paul Dano’s frightening take as the Riddler and Colin Farrell’s scene-stealing Penguin.

Reeves also presented the best-ever take on Gotham City. While Burton’s Gotham was bold and interesting, neither of his films made Gotham feel like a real city. Nolan’s Gotham, with its unconcealed usage of Chicago and New York City, had little personality at all. But Reeves’ Gotham felt like a broken city that desperately needed a Batman to come to its rescue, with production combining parts of Chicago and Scotland to give the city a more naturalistic, gothic vibe. The film’s nearly three-hour runtime only assisted in the viewer’s immersion into Reeves’ Gotham.

But three hours in Reeves’ Gotham still wasn’t enough. The Batman Part II, which seems to be telling a story with Two-Face as a villain, was delayed for years due to a personal issue going on with Matt Reeves. Filming finally began in June, but in early October, Reeves had to step away, most likely for the same family issue. Steven Soderbergh then stepped in to finish the filming Reeves began, which is currently ongoing. While I applaud Reeves for prioritizing his family over a film, this means that it will be six years between The Batman and its sequel and it's hard to imagine that Skydance, the new owners of DC Studios, will want to keep Batman on the shelf for that long again, nor do I think they’ll want to schedule around Pattinson’s Bond films.

And, while it never seemed like a real possibility, I was still hoping that Pattinson would get folded into what James Gunn and Peter Safran had been building for the DCU if only because I don’t think it's all that easy to keep finding great guys to be Batman, and while he wasn’t quite as good as Bale — or even Keaton — Pattinson truly is/was a great Batman. It’s just too bad that his take on the character will be cut short before we get a proper trilogy.

Report: Cowboys RG Tyler Booker has a high-ankle sprain

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Cowboys may be without right guard Tyler Booker in the coming weeks.

Booker left Sunday’s 46-40 loss to the 49ers after playing 33 offensive snaps and Ian Rapoport of NFL Media reports that he is dealing with a high-ankle sprain.

High-ankle sprains often lead to multi-week absences and Booker missed three games with a similar injury last season. The Cowboys face the Titans, Eagles and Seahawks before a bye in Week 14.

T.J. Bass took over for the final 50 snaps of Sunday’s loss and will likely be at right guard until Booker is cleared to return to action.

We’ve Got a New James Bond, and He’s No Stranger to Major Film Franchises

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Robert Pattinson is staying booked and busy.

It’s safe to say actor Robert Pattinson is on a generational run, starring in films The Odyssey, The Drama, Die My Love and Primetime –  just this year alone. Having previously played Edward Cullen in The Twilight Saga and Bruce Wayne in Matt Reeves’ The Batman (and gearing up for a role in Dune: Part III this December), he’s no stranger to major film franchises.

And now he’s adding one more major role to his portfolio: James Bond.

Amazon MGM Studios named the English actor the new 007 on Monday, ending the hunt to replace Daniel Craig, who played Bond from 2006 to 2021 in five films: Casino Royale, Quantum of Solace, Skyfall, Spectre and No Time to Die.

Read More: Ready to Watch a James Bond Movie? These Are My Top Recommendations for You

Several other British A-list actors were speculated to be in the running for the role, including Jack Lowden, Callum Turner and Josh O’Connor, but it seems Amazon MGM Studios is banking on a big-name actor with a proven record of box-office success for their lead. Pattinson’s connection to director Denis Villeneuve (also director of the Dune trilogy) likely played a role in the decision, too.

“To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement,” Pattinson said in a statement. “I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Pattinson is the eighth actor to play James Bond. The full list of actors who have played Bond on the big screen includes Sean Connery (1962-1983), David Niven for one film in 1967, George Lazenby for one film in 1969, Roger Moore (1973-1985), Timothy Dalton (1987-1989), Pierce Brosnan (1995-2002) and Craig (2006-2021).

The next Bond film has not yet been titled and is not expected to begin filming until 2027, with a 2028 release date. Steven Knight (Peaky Blinders) has been brought on as the screenwriter, with Amy Pascal (Project Hail Mary) and David Heyman (Harry Potter series) producing and Tanya Lapointe (Dune trilogy) executive producing.

Amazon formally closed its acquisition of MGM Studios in 2022 for $8.45 billion, giving it the rights to distribute all James Bond films. In 2025, Amazon MGM Studios announced a joint venture with longtime James Bond franchise producers Michael G. Wilson and Barbara Broccoli to remain co-owners of the franchise, while ultimately giving Amazon MGM Studios creative control.

“Few films carry the kind of anticipation that comes with James Bond, and we have strived to honor the passionate fans, the legacy and the future of this iconic character,” Courtenay Valenti, head of film at Amazon MGM Studios, said. “We are thrilled to officially welcome Robert Pattinson as our 007 and are confident he will bring something truly special to this role. Robert, Denis, Amy, David and Tanya are embarking on an amazing adventure together.”

The Chevy El Camino Launched In 1959, And This Is The Ute's Most Sought-After Model Year

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No, the Chevy El Camino wasn't the first to combine a two-door car with an open bed truck. The first production vehicle to do that was a Ford, but possibly not the one you're thinking of. A design engineer named Lew Bandt, then with Ford of Australia, developed what's often considered the original factory-made ute — the 1934 Ford Model 40-A Light Delivery. Depending on exactly how you define a ute, some folks point to the 1927 Volvo OV4 as well.

Of course, it was the 1957 Ford Ranchero that truly inspired the El Camino. The Ranchero was built on the Blue Oval's full-size platform of the time, and it became both relatively popular and relatively profitable for the automaker. In fact, it was successful enough that Chevy was inspired to get in on the ute action with its full-size 1959 El Camino. Originally a short-lived model only in production for that year and 1960, the El Camino would get a second chance at life in 1964. That's when the Bowtie brand revived the name and brought it back in slightly downsized form on the same platform as the intermediate-class Chevrolet Chevelle.

This is where things start to get interesting for gearheads. In 1970, Chevrolet introduced a 454-cubic-inch LS6 V8 that was reserved for both the Chevelle and its platform mate. With that mill standing out as the most powerful unit of the year, the former became a muscle-car legend and one of Dom Toretto's rides in the epic 10-movie "Fast and Furious" saga. Meanwhile, the latter turned into perhaps the most sought-after El Camino of them all, the 1970 El Camino SS LS6 454. Keep in mind that this was a whole different engine than the LS6 in the 200-mph 2027 Chevrolet Corvette Stingray.

There are two key reasons that enthusiasts crave this machine. Right off the bat, the 1970 El Camino SS 454 was a one-year wonder — its exact powerplant was the aggressive LS6 V8 that would be dropped from the El Camino for 1971. True, Chevy sold a 1971 El Camino SS 454, but that mill was the less-powerful LS5. As far as actual production numbers for the 1970 model, those are hard to come by. Hemmings says 4,475 Chevrolets in total were sold with the LS6 for that year, but they were spread out among both Chevelles and El Caminos.

The second major selling point here is the outrageous driving experience offered by these rides, and for that, credit also goes to their incredible engine. Factory rated at 450 horsepower with a whopping 500 pound-feet of torque, this motor featured a cast-iron block and heads along with an 11.25:1 compression ratio. Also adding to the muscle are a four-bolt main block, a four-barrel Holley carburetor, and a high-performance camshaft. The outcome was that both the El Camino and Chevelle could serve up more horses than not just the 1970 Corvette, but all other production cars, too.

As further proof of just how hard people are jonesing for the 1970 El Camino SS LS6 454, an example may hold the record for the highest auction price for any El Camino — the car went for $275,000 at a Barrett-Jackson sale in 2023. Just remember auction values can change quickly: one 1970 Chevelle LS6 lost its owner a million dollars.

All the Red-Carpet Looks From the 2026 Governors Awards

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Governors Awards are back. The annual gala hosted by the Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences (who also hosts the Oscars) to honor lifetime achievement and extraordinary contributions to film has returned for its 17th edition—and the red carpet is brimming with star power.

While this gala itself is not aired live, like the main Oscars ceremony (which takes place in March every year), the awards given are considered genuine Oscars. Until 2009, the awards now given at the Governors Awards were presented live during the main Oscars telecast, but the Academy separated them into their own gala—both to shorten the main television broadcast and to give honorees more time to celebrate their wins and present their speeches.

Last year, Tom Cruise, Debbie Allen, and Wynn Thomas received Honorary Oscars, while Dolly Parton was given the Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award. On the red carpet, we saw everyone, from Jennifer Lawrence to Anya Taylor-Joy, Cynthia Erivo, and Dakota Johnson.

Meanwhile, tonight’s honorees include Glenn Close, Ridley Scott, and Floyd Norman—who are all receiving Honorary Oscars—alongside producers Christine Vachon and Pamela Koffler, who will receive the Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award.

But there are plenty of other guests in attendance this evening at the the Ray Dolby Ballroom at Ovation Hollywood in Los Angeles, and many more will continue to roll in until the ceremony kicks off at 9 p.m. ET.

Ben Affleck Slammed For "Sexuality" Question By Star While Filming Kevin Smith's Classic Satire

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

An avid comic book fan since she first read "Stan Lee's Soapbox," Allison Hambrick is your friendly neighborhood generalist writer. Journalism, editing, media production, copywriting—she does it all. From her very first blog post about Smallville as a primary schooler, Allison developed a passion for superheroes and the entertainment industry at large.

Majoring in writing and film at SCAD, Allison put her skills to work writing, editing, podcasting, and more. Currently, she moonlights as an editor at the ASHRAE Journal by day and Screen Rant by night. Allison also previously worked for Warner Bros. Entertainment and for Disney.

Through her writing, she hopes to inspire, to entertain, and to empower others to be the best versions of themselves, and her mission in life is to make Stan proud. In her free time, she enjoys reading, video game design, traveling, and spending time outside with her dogs, Loki and Elphaba. Allison is fluent in movie quotes, and her favorite film is Superman II.

Simultaneously Kevin Smith's most irreverent and deepest film, Dogma remains a poignant critique on Catholicism two decades later.

Released in 1999, the film stars Linda Fiorentino as Bethany Sloane, the last scion and a good-hearted abortion clinic counselor. She is recruited by the Metatron (Alan Rickman) to help stop two fallen angels, Loki (Matt Damon) and Bartleby (Ben Affleck), from entering a Catholic church and receiving forgiveness. Doing so would allow them re-entry into Heaven upon death, thus challenging God's omnipotence and causing a world-erasing apocalypse.

Bethany is aided on her journey by the 13th apostle Rufus (Chris Rock), the muse Serendipity (Salma Hayek), and two prophets, Jay and Silent Bob (Jason Mewes and Smith). The film's denouement even features iconic singer Alanis Morissette as God herself. While Dogma received mostly positive reviews, it has largely been mired in criticism, both over its satire of religion and a rights dispute between Smith and the disgraced Harvey Weinstein.

Though she did not speak on the movie itself, Morissette recently called out Affleck for an inappropriate remark he made to her during a concert. While relaying the struggles she faced during the early days of her music career, she candidly revealed that she's "even had a guy ask [her] why [she] didn’t lead with [her] sexuality." Then, as a clip of Affleck's head exploding in Dogma played, Morissette said, "Thank you, Ben Affleck."

Morissette's story, though recounted humorously, shows a troubling reality many women face in the entertainment industry, one underscored when remembering that Dogma was produced by Weinstein. In spite of the many controversies associated with the movie, Smith confirmed last year that he will be creating a sequel. What's more, Affleck is attached to return, despite his character's grisly fate.

As for how that is possible, Smith had a simple answer: Bartleby and Loki are in Hell. "After Dogma came out, people would be like, ‘What happens to Bartleby and Loki at the end?'" Smith explained. "And I was like, well, if you’re a touchy-feely Christian, you believe in forgiveness. But if you’re an old-school Catholic like myself, they’re burning in hell. They broke the laws, and that’s what happens. There’s punishment there. So that plays into our follow-up."

Morissette is not confirmed to return as God, and her negative opinion of Affleck may not bode well for a reunion. However, she has collaborated with Smith and Mewes since Dogma, with all three appearing on the same episode of Degrassi: The Next Generation in 2005. As such, Morissette may be game for another run, especially if she gets to explode Affleck's head once more.

Marvel Officially Confirms Ryan Reynolds and Hugh Jackman for ‘Avengers: Doomsday’

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

Ryan Reynolds showed up to Marvel’s Avengers: Doomsday panel at San Diego Comic-Con dressed as “Jeanpool” and asked whether there was still time to get into the movie. By then, filming had wrapped, so he started asking about additional photography. It was a good bit from an actor who knows exactly how closely people listen when he talks about Deadpool. Months later, his name has turned up somewhere more concrete than a Comic-Con stage.

Hugh Jackman has given Marvel fans just as much reason to watch for his name. The Doomsday lineup already includes several of his former X-Men co-stars, including Sir Patrick Stewart, Ian McKellen, and James Marsden, as well as Channing Tatum back as Gambit after making his debut in Deadpool & Wolverine. The latest update comes from the Writers Guild of America, which gives both Jackman and Reynolds a place in the movie’s credits.

Reynolds and Jackman are credited with “Additional Literary Material” on Avengers: Doomsday, according to the WGA listing. Stephen McFeely and Michael Waldron share the movie’s “Written by” credit. The guild listing confirms that Reynolds and Jackman worked on the movie in a writing capacity. Marvel has yet to announce either actor as part of the cast.

Four villains, and four arguments the films were honest enough to actually let them make. Here is the one you lean towards, the part of it that holds up, and the exact point where it stops holding up.

You align with the one who was right about the problem and wrong about the solution. Killmonger is the most persuasive villain Marvel has produced because the film never pretends his anger came from nowhere.

You align with the protector, which is the most understandable and most dangerous position on this list. Namor is not chasing power or revenge, he is doing whatever keeps his people hidden, and he has been doing it for centuries.

You align with the patient one, the only man on this list with no powers, no army and no hurry. Zemo lost his family in Sokovia and then spent years working out that you do not fight the Avengers, you introduce them to each other's secrets.

You align with the one who decided the answer was arithmetic. Thanos is not angry and not grieving in any way he will admit to, he simply reached a conclusion and never once revisited it, which is precisely what makes him frightening.

When Deadpool & Wolverine began filming during the 2023 writers’ strike, Reynolds had to stick to the script he’d helped write, because coming up with a new line for Deadpool between takes would have counted as writing during the strike. The movie kept shooting under those restrictions until the SAG-AFTRA strike halted production in July. Reynolds had already co-written Deadpool 2, so there’s a precedent for him helping shape what ends up on the page. For Jackman, however, the Doomsday listing is a first after more than two decades of playing Wolverine.

The Writers Guild of America uses “Additional Literary Material” to recognize contributions from writers who worked on a movie and don’t share its principal writing credit. The listing leaves open what Reynolds and Jackman wrote, whose scenes they worked on, and what made it into the finished movie. As we already knew, Marvel’s November 12 cast guide already names Tatum’s Gambit and several of Jackman’s former X-Men co-stars. Deadpool and Wolverine, notably, are still the two names fans are waiting to see on that list.

Avengers: Doomsday opens in theaters on December 18, 2026.

Deleted Batman Scene Gets Official Update From Clayface Director

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: The DC Universe has started planting the seeds for its take on the Batman mythology.

The DCU's Chapter 1: "Gods and Monsters" has brought numerous characters to life in James Gunn's franchise, with Superman and Supergirl taking it to the big screen, while HBO's Lanterns expanded upon the Emerald Knights' place in the continuity. With 2026 reaching its end, DC Studios took audience members to Gotham City to explore a famous foe in Bruce Wayne's mythology.

In an exclusive interview with ScreenRant's Andy Behbakht, Clayface director James Watkins spoke about the R-rated release in the DCU. While The Dark Knight may not have appeared, the filmmaker was asked about Tom Rhys Harries' previous comments that there was seemingly some footage with Batman, when he and his co-star were asked by ComicBook if there were discussions about whether Matt Hagen and Dr. Caitlyn Corr were aware of the Gotham City crimefighter.

The Clayface star teased at the time, "We have footage. I don't know how much I can say, but yes, we had discussions about it at length." When posed with the actor's quote from October, Watkins shared the following when asked if he could elaborate on what that Batman tease was, whether it had been a brief glimpse at the Caped Crusader through a magazine, newspaper headline, or something else:

James Watkins: I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I think the closest we had was things [like] Easter eggs that the art department might have dropped in, in terms of posters in the background or elements of design or chairs from various old versions of Batman, things like that. One of the things that appealed to me most about Mike's original script was the fact that it was a standalone story, and to what you said at the beginning, I know we've got the DC logo on this; this film's open to everybody.

You don't need to have a sort of deep knowledge of the background in order to come in and enjoy it. It's just one person's story within Gotham. Much like Penguin's show or The Joker [movie,] it's very much standalone. And I don't think in the journey of Matt into Clayface and his sort of villainous journey, he's yet really reached those levels where he would've possibly arrived on Batman's radar in a really significant way.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

The Clayface movie had its fair share of Easter eggs, and during production, there had been additional nods to the Batman mythology spotted through set photos and videos. Throughout the film, the story included elements such as The Gotham Gazette and the GCPD.

During principal photography, there was the Joker Easter egg as a poster said, "Corruption is the enemy of the people, march against the GCPD. Join the Jokers," but there were also teases to one of the famous Boy Wonders. A Flying Graysons poster was spotted, serving as a big hint to the first Robin, Dick Grayson, but in the end, the theatrical cut never features any of them.

Even though they are never seen in Clayface, ScreenRant's Andy Behbakht asked Watkins if they might be featured on the home media release, and whether or not those nods were a deliberate choice or if they came from the art department, as more Batman characters get added to the DCU. The filmmaker gave the following response:

James Watkins: I mean, the person to talk to is James Price because the graphics department were always coming to me and going, "Look, we've got this tiny thing in the back of the shot." Literally it's like, "Here's a poster or here's a sticker that we've put on a phone box in the background." And I'm like, "Okay, is that going to read?" I think there are probably things in there that are so deeply buried, or once you get onto high definition, everything you can zoom in and you can probably see. I mean, I'd have to talk about it with James properly and go, "Okay, James, what actually..." Because I wasn't focused on, particularly, trying to smuggle stuff in, but I know they always weren't. It was kind of a running joke. It's like, 'Yeah, we've snuck something in the back of your shop."

5 Brilliant Books To Read Before Kicking the Bucket

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Five stories that are bound to make a vivid impression

On principle, any list of “books you must read before you die” should be unending. Literary staples aside, the number of must-read books in existence is constantly expanding and evolving. Older books undergo revaluation, no-brainer entries are viewed through a fresh lens, and contemporary titles join an already massive library of must-reads.

Also, tastes are subjective. Not everyone might jive with Great Expectations or Les Misérables despite their objective artistic merit. The definition of “must-read” has drastically changed over time. Keeping this in mind, Polygon has chosen five brilliant, indisputable books to read before kicking the bucket. Some are obvious picks, while others, not so much.

Fyodor Dostoevsky’s body of work is often classified as essential reads for good reason, and The Brothers Karamazov is undoubtedly one such entry. The author’s final novel was written under dire personal circumstances, which included failing health and profound emotional grief. The novel is deeply philosophical in nature, raising pertinent questions about faith, morality, and skepticism. From a certain perspective, it's also about human evil, and how it manifests as mundane emotions like greed or hedonism.

The novel opens with wealthy landowner Fyodor Karamazov neglecting all three of his sons — Dmitri, Ivan, and Alyosha. Dostoevsky goes into intricate detail to flesh out the characters in this dysfunctional household, which is caught up in an inheritance dispute and subsequent scandal. After a murder takes place, The Brothers Karamazov turns into a tense courtroom drama. Each Karamazov brother represents a human archetype, which Dostoevsky uses to debate the nature of free will. While this book isn’t as relentless and claustrophobic as Crime and Punishment, it is sweeping and dramatic in its exploration of character motivations and human behavior.

A milestone in African literature, Chinua Achebe’s Things Fall Apart is about Okonkwo, an influential leader of the fictional Igbo clan. Okonkwo is an accomplished warrior and champion who vows to be nothing like his alcoholic, flute-playing father. After he takes a young boy named Ikemefuna as his ward, the oracle of his village declares the child must be killed. This incident haunts Okonkwo, who goes on to oppose rampant colonialism and reclaim the lost dignity of his people. Achebe presents an essential viewpoint, as most colonial literature portrayed Igbo culture in biased and problematic ways.

Things Fall Apart provides rich complexity to the tribes portrayed, granting an authentic look into local customs, laws, and traditions. Okonkwo is a deeply flawed but heroic protagonist who tries to hold onto tradition in the light of aggressive socio-political change. Few novels dissect colonialism like Achebe’s debut story, which explains how colonizers quietly infiltrate local cultures and turn close-knit communities against one another. This leads to a fractured sense of shared history and heritage that requires an enormous amount of time to acclimatize to.

Arundhati Roy’s most famous work, The God of Small Things, is an unflinching look at vulnerability in the face of rigid societal rules. The novel follows fraternal twins Estha and Rahel, who grow up amid the arrival of their British cousin in India and intense familial tensions. Roy uses a disjointed narrative to tell this moving tale about social discrimination, colonialism, and local politics that inevitably affect the personal lives of our protagonists. The prose is vivid and sensory, filtered through the perspective of children who view the world in a more tactile way than adults do.

The God of Small Things pits massive historical upheavals against unforgettable personal events, including the fleeting beauty of growing up and the wonder of young love. While focusing on these tender moments, Roy also critiques casteist social practices that prevent the romantic union between a divorced woman and a carpenter. The brutality of British rule goes hand in hand with these quiet tragedies, which Roy explores by weaving in and out of time to showcase how our memories work. It is a haunting book that radically reinvents language to convey the hyperspecific feeling of losing childhood innocence in a postcolonial world.

The book that established the dark academia subgenre, Donna Tartt’s The Secret History is a riveting campus novel that everybody should experience. Set in an elite liberal arts college in Vermont, the book follows Richard Papen, who joins an exclusive Classics and Greek class in a bid to fit in. The other members of the class are all wealthy and hand-picked by the professor, and revel in their social isolation, which they consider proof of their intellectual superiority. After one of them is murdered, The Secret History turns into an inverted detective story that dwells on Richard’s perspective of the other students, and the effect that this death has on everybody.

Tartt doesn’t spring this death on the reader. Instead, the book opens with the declaration of the murder. The tension lies in slowly unraveling why this happened, and how the others are responsible for such a ghastly event. Richard is also an unreliable narrator, being a self-confessed liar and someone who blatantly alters objective truth due to his biases. Tartt uses layered, evocative prose to explore morality via the lens of Greek ideals, penning a cautionary tale against the romanticization of intellectual elitism. The bubble that surrounds these students bursts, pushing them towards real-world culpability they’re not equipped to handle.

Humans share a complicated relationship with their sense of smell, detailed vividly in Patrick Süskind’s Perfume: The Story of a Murderer. The story follows Jean-Baptiste Grenouille, an orphan in 18th-century France who is born with an exceptional nose that can differentiate between subtle scents around him. While this gift initially pulls Grenouille towards becoming a perfumer, things take a horrific turn after he meets a young girl with a scent that’s impossible to forget. What starts off as intense fascination turns into a morbid obsession with human scent, which he hunts in order to make the most exquisite perfume.

Perfume paints Grenouille as a fascinating antagonist with no scent of his own. Süskind uses his journey as a metaphor for human morality and desire, and how easy it is to be manipulated by factors like profound alienation. A person with no empathetic roots in the world will treat humans like fodder, as Grenouille has never been loved as a child and never grew to discover the sentiment. This is a chilling portrait of a monster whose story ends in a bizarre, haunting way that will leave you stunned.
