Lotus L. Kang wins Canada’s top art prize, the Sobey Art Award

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lotus L. Kang, winner of the 2026 Sobey Art Award Photo: National Gallery of Canada, Ottawa

The National Gallery of Canada (NGC) and the Sobey Art Foundation named the artist Lotus L. Kang asthe winner of the 2026 Sobey Art Award during a ceremony on Saturday (14 November). The Toronto-born, New York-based photographer, sculptor and installation artist won the 23rd edition of the annual award, the most high-profile and lucrative prize devoted to Canadian contemporary artists, and will receive C$100,000 ($70,000).

The other five shortlisted artists—Melaw Nakehk'o (from the Circumpolar region), Samuel Roy-Bois (Pacific), Audie Murray (Prairies), Caroline Monnet (Québec) and Shane Perley-Dutcher (Atlantic)—will each receive C$25,000 ($17,500), while the 24 longlisted artists each received C$10,000 ($7,000), bringing the total value of the award to C$465,000 ($325,800).

In a statement, Jonathan Shaughnessy, the chair of the jury for this year’s Sobey Art Award, praised Kang for “the commitment and rigour demonstrated in her practice for well over a decade”. He continued: “Through installations using unfixed photographic film, cast and found objects, and fabricated armatures, her practice resists the stabilising impulses traditionally associated with photography. Reoccurring motifs including lotus root, flowers, soju bottles, birds, anchovies, are not fixed objects, but repositories of place, longing and desire.”

Partial view of Lotus L. Kang's installation In Cascades (2023-24) in Whitney Biennial 2024: Even Better Than the Real Thing at the Whitney Museum of American Art Photo: The Art Newspaper

Kang, a recipient of a Guggenheim Fellowship in 2024 whose work was included in the 2024 Whitney Biennial and the 2025 Shanghai Biennale, works across sculpture, photography and site-responsive installation. Her materially dense, non-linear installations explore the liminal space between industrial and architectural forms, familial and social histories, as well as poetry and non-human figures. One of her celebrated installation works, Mother (2020), comprised dozens of metal mixing bowls filled with scattered abstract clay, rubber and stone sculptures as well as aluminium casts of fruit and anchovies. Her critically acclaimed solo exhibition at 52 Walker in New York last year featured several greenhouse installations as well as one made of strips of film extended around a spinning rotary dryer.

“It is an honour to be recognised by this prize,” Kang said in a statement. “I am grateful to those I love, those who love me and those who have supported me; you keep me going. The recognition of this prize means that askew ideas are worth pursuing. Art is essential to life, progress and love.”

During the ceremony, Kang told those assembled at the NGC and those watching via livestream: “I just want to congratulate the other artists. It’s an honour to be nominated alongside you. Shane, Melaw, Caroline, Audie, Samuel. It is really inspiring to be showing alongside you, and who knows what would have happened, and I’m just glad we’ll all keep making art, because it’s really the best thing. So, thank you so much.’’

Lotus L. Kang, Receiver Transmitter (49 Echoes I), 2022-25 © Lotus Kang. Photo: Courtesy of the artist and 52 Walker gallery, New York

Rob Sobey, the chair of the Sobey Art Foundation, offered his “heartfelt congratulations” to Kang and added: “We also celebrate tonight all 29 artists on this year’s longlist and shortlist for their creativity and vision. We are proud to champion their work and grateful to the National Gallery of Canada for helping bring these remarkable artistic voices to audiences at home and around the world.”

Selected by an independent jury of curators and cultural leaders, Kang was chosen following a rigorous review process. The jury, who evaluated the artists’ practices, achievements, and contributions to contemporary art in Canada included Kimberly Phillips, the director of the Marianne and Edward Gibson Art Museum at Simon Fraser University, representing the Pacific region;Daisy Desrosiers, a curator at large for North America at Tate, representing the Quebec region;and Helen Nisbet, the director of Glasgow International, serving as international juror.

Works by all six shortlisted artists are currently featured in the 2026 Sobey Art Award Exhibition at the NGC in Ottawa (until 3 January 2027).

The late Frank H. Sobey, an enthusiastic collector of Canadian art, established the Sobey Art Foundation in 1981 and the Sobey Art Award was launched in 2002. Five years ago, the award’s criteria were amended from a prize devoted to “emerging artists” under 40 to recognising artists of any age who are at a “critical juncture” in their careers. Past winners have included Brian Jungen (in 2002), Annie Pootoogook (2006), Abbas Akhavan (2015), Laakkuluk Williamson Bathory (2021) and Nico Williams (2024).

From an initial field of 30 nominees, a shortlist of just six artists is now competing for the prize

The British Columbia-based artist, who is a member of the Secwépemc First Nation, described the experience of taking home the C$100,000 Sobey Art Award as “a waking dream”

The six shortlisted artists, each representing a different region, will be featured in an exhibition opening at the National Gallery of Canada in September

Thirty artists are nominated for the Sobey Art Award this year, with the total purse for the award growing to C$465,000

Russell Brand’s Wife Breaks Silence on His Conviction, Prison Sentence

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Russell Brand's wife Laura Brand shared her first public comments since her husband was sentenced to 10 years in prison after being convicted of rape and sexual assault.

Russell Brand’s wife Laura Brand is adjusting to a new reality.

Days after the British comedian was sentenced to over 10 years in prison following his rape and sexual assault conviction, his wife spoke out in her first comments since the news, sharing the fate of their weekly faith podcast Sunday Service with The Brands.

“I’m here to continue the good work that was being established here in this Sunday Service community,” Laura told the camera on the Nov. 15 episode. “Something very close to mine and Russell's hearts.

“It may look different,” she continued, “but the ambitions are very much the same.”

The 39-year-old—who shares three kids with Russell—admitted that she’s turning to her faith as she moves forward with the show without her husband.

“We trust God, and we trust his plan, and we trust everything He’s doing right now, even though it’s painful,” Laura shared. “And obviously [it] will take some getting used to for me to even sit here and not have my companion and my person with me.”

After thanking fans for their “support,” the mom of three noted that she’s been speaking with Russell.

Laura’s video comes on the heels of Russell’s conviction and prison sentencing.

On Nov. 5, the 51-year-old—who had pleaded guilty to all charges—was convicted by a London court on one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault for events that allegedly occurred between 1999 and 2009.

Although Russell was convicted of four charges, he was cleared of two separate counts of rape and one count of indecent assault.

However, days later, on Nov. 9, judge Joel Bennathan sentenced Russell to 10 years and six months in prison, according to The Telegraph.

While the Forgetting Sarah Marshall actor’s sentencing concludes his case that began in April 2025, his legal battle may not have reached its end.

Following Russell’s Nov. 5 conviction, the Metropolitan Police of London confirmed to E! News an investigation into the former reality TV presenter is still ongoing after two additional allegations of rape were brought forward.

“The suspect has previously been interviewed under caution in connection with the allegations,” the statement noted. “Files have been submitted to the Crown Prosecution Service in relation to both complainants. Detectives remain in close contact with prosecutors as enquiries continue and further investigative work is carried out.”

For a detailed look back at Brand’s trial, keep reading…

Russell Brand was charged with rape and sexual assault in April 2025.

The London Metropolitan Police charged Brand for one count of rape, two counts of indecent assault and one count of indecent assault in April 2025 for events that transpired between 1999 and 2005.

At the time, Brand immediately denied the allegations in a video response, noting he was receiving “egregious attacks” from the media in addition to the allegations.

“Amidst this litany of astonishing, rather baroque attacks are some very serious allegations,” Brand said in a video shared to social media at the time, “that I absolutely refute.”

Russell Brand pleaded not guilty to all charges.

Though the Forgetting Sarah Marshall star had previously written in a 2007 essay for The Guardian that he had a “sexy addiction” and “disregard for social conventions of sexual protocol,” he vehemently denied the charges against him.

In his video statement, Brand admitted he had been a “fool” in the past, although maintained he never partook in “non-consensual activity.”

“I was a fool before I lived in the light of the Lord,” Brand said in a video shared to his X account in April 2025. “I was a drug addict, a sex addict and an imbecile, but what I never was, was a rapist.”

In the same video, Brand noted he was “grateful” to have the opportunity to fight the allegations in court.  He pleaded not guilty to all five charges in May 2025.

Russell Brand was served with two more sexual offense charges in December 2025.

With the additional allegations, Brand was charged with three counts of rape, three counts of sexual assault and one count of indecent assault involving six women for events that transpired between 1999 and 2009. He later pleaded not guilty to the additional charges.

Russell Brand was found guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault.

In a November 2026 ruling, a London court found Brand guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault. He was cleared of two additional counts of rape and one count of indecent assault.

During the trial, Brand maintained his innocence and said that while he dedicated much of his life to “beyond reckless philandering,” per the New York Times, he never engaged in non-consensual activities.

Russell Brand was sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison.

After confirming he would sentence the Get Him to the Greek alum with “significant custodial time,” during the ruling and denying him bail, presiding judge Joel Bennathan sentenced Brand to 10 years and six months in prison.

During the Nov. 9, 2026 sentencing, Bennathan called Brand a “sexual predator,” and commended the women who testified for their “determination and bravery.”

Bennathan said he considered Brand’s family in his ruling, calling his decision a “sad loss” for Brand’s three children, whom he shares with wife Laura Brand.

However, the judge noted that Brand had brought the decision “upon himself,” noting the comedian had acted with “casual, arrogant disregard,” for women.

Russell Brand legal team said he is a “fundamentally different person” now.

Prior to the sentencing, Brand’s lawyer Ian Winter KC said his client is “fundamentally a different person today,” than he was when the incidents took place, per CBS News.

Winter noted that the rape for which Brand was found guilty—which took place in 2009—was a “momentary total failure.”

Brand is also represented by Oliver Schneider-Sikorsky. His legal team will have the opportunity to appeal.

James Bond Finds Its Perfect Replacement in This 6-Episode Miniseries

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Espionage stories have fascinated audiences for generations, from the cool precision of classic John le Carré adaptations and the legacy of James Bond to modern hits like The Americans and Slow Horses. Even when they lean into action or stylized intrigue, there’s a certain comfort in knowing the danger is usually a fictional, heightened version of spycraft built for entertainment. But real-world espionage carries a very different kind of weight and may leave you questioning even your closest allies.

That’s exactly what makes A Spy Among Friends resonate. Based on true events and adapted from Ben Macintyre’s acclaimed book about British intelligence officer Kim Philby, the six-episode miniseries leans into the inner workings and psychological pressure of espionage rather than the slick, explosive elements the genre often relies on. Instead of battling enemies across borders, the series reveals that the most devastating betrayals come from within in one of the biggest intelligence scandals of the 20th century.

A Spy Among Friends tells the story of the decades-long relationship between Nicholas Elliott (Damian Lewis), a well-respected MI6 officer, and his closest friend Kim Philby (Guy Pearce), who is eventually unmasked as one of the most notorious double agents in history. The series frames their friendship as both a source of deep loyalty and a blind spot, which allowed Philby to operate as a Soviet mole for years without arousing Elliott’s suspicion. When Philby finally defects to Moscow, the British government launches an inquiry to determine whether Elliott was simply deceived or whether he might have been helping his friend all along.

The series begins in the aftermath of Philby’s exposure, sending Elliott to Beirut with the mission of extracting a confession from a man he'd known intimately for over 30 years. Over the course of four tense days and 34 hours of taped conversations, the truth slips further out of reach, culminating in Philby’s defection to the USSR. Back in London, Elliott faces his own interrogation at the hands of MI5 officer Lily Thomas (Anna Maxwell Martin), one of the show’s few fictional additions. Through her relentless questioning, the series unravels Elliott’s complicated mix of loyalty, denial, and self-preservation.

What ultimately sets A Spy Among Friends apart is its refusal to treat Philby’s treachery as a standard espionage mystery filled with high-octane twists and turns. Instead, it focuses on the emotional wreckage left behind when a deeply personal relationship becomes the site of catastrophic betrayal. Pearce and Lewis play off each other with remarkable precision, their performances grounding the series in the uncomfortable truth that the deepest betrayals rarely come from enemies abroad, but from the people we think we know the best.

As compelling as the story is on its own, A Spy Among Friends reaches another level thanks to the exceptional performances at its center. Damian Lewis came into prominence with his Emmy-winning role in Homeland, playing a man constantly walking the line between loyalty and deception, and later carried that same complexity into Billions. His portrayal of Nicholas Elliott taps into a similar tension. While Elliott is clearly devastated by Philby’s betrayal, there’s always an undercurrent suggesting he may have let his personal affection cloud his professional judgment. Lewis plays him with a quiet, tightly coiled restraint that makes Elliot’s internal crisis feel both painfully raw and deeply contained.

Guy Pearce, meanwhile, is mesmerizing as Kim Philby. His villainous turns have always had a distinctive nuance, from the cold, calculating betrayal he brought to The Count of Monte Cristo to the wounded arrogance of Iron Man 3. But here, he delivers something far more unsettling. Pearce resists the temptation to portray Philby as a conventional villain, instead capturing the strange magnetism that allowed Philby to manipulate everyone around him. The series keeps Philby’s motivations intentionally ambiguous, and Pearce leans into that uncertainty, playing him as someone who may genuinely believe his own lies. It’s the kind of performance that makes it entirely plausible that this man deceived governments — and his closest friends — for decades.

Who was Kim Philby, the "spy who betrayed a generation"?

The writing and structure of the series amplify these performances. Each episode peels back another layer, shifting across timelines while gradually revealing which memories are reliable and which have been warped by guilt, ego, or denial. Showrunner Alexander Cary, who wrote all six episodes, uses this fractured narrative to recreate the emotional fog that defined Elliott and Philby’s friendship. And while true stories can sometimes slip into the dryness of a history lesson, A Spy Among Friends never does. Its character-first writing and dialogue-driven scenes give Lewis and Pearce the room to turn every exchange into a captivating, high-stakes moment, grounding real events in raw, intimate human drama rather than procedural detail.

A Spy Among Friends isn’t the kind of spy thriller that relies on spectacle, and that’s exactly why it works so well. It’s a reminder that real espionage is far more personal than what movies like James Bond often make it out to be. Anchored by magnetic performances from Lewis and Pearce, the miniseries offers a darker, more realistic portrait of spycraft, one where friendship becomes a liability and trust is never guaranteed. For fans of character-driven espionage rooted in real history, A Spy Among Friends is definitely worth watching.

Zootopia 3 Release Timeline Addressed By Disney Boss After 9-Year Sequel Wait

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Zootopia 3's future gets an official update.

The Zootopia franchise began in 2016 with the release of the first movie created by directors Byron Howard, Rich Moore, and Jared Bush. The filmmakers spent a year researching real animals throughout Disney's Animal Kingdom in Kenya and built the franchise around a mammal metropolis where predators and prey coexist. Since it debuted, the franchise has exploded in popularity with sequels, games, theme park attractions, and streaming series. Now, a third installment is making its way into theaters.

Zootopia 3 is currently in development and there has been little announced about what to expect from the third installment of the beloved animated franchise. In an interview with Collider, Bush explored what fans of the movies can expect from Zootopia 3, as well as sharing a big update on the release schedule following a nine-year wait between Zootopia and its sequel Zootopia 2. When asked about the development process for the third movie, Bush shared:

Bush: This is something that is going to take a lot of time to get right. I think with any movie set in the world of Zootopia, Judy and Nick are going to be at the very center of that. I think people know at this point that we are going to introduce birds into the world of Zootopia, and I think this may be the first time that we travel outside the bounds of Zootopia City itself.

Collider: Has this moved up the chart? Because I think you have a 10-year schedule or a five-year schedule.

It has previously been announced that birds will make their way into Zootopia, which has never been shown before during the animated franchise. Nick and Judy will continue to be the main characters spearheading the upcoming installment, with fans still hoping for a romance between the two characters. When asked about how long fans can expect to wait for Zootopia 3 like sticking to a 10-year to five-year schedule, Bush stated:

Bush: Here’s something that I’ve found, being at Disney Animation. When a movie is ready, that’s when it goes out into the world. And so I think that we have general ideas of when something might be ready, but until you’re in there, until everyone’s watching the movie and we all go, “This is the very best that we can make this. Now it’s ready to go out into the world,” that’s really what determines our release schedule. Roughly, we have to have a sense of a musical to a nonmusical so we’re not bunching those up, or an original to a sequel story. We like to vary those quite a bit. So, there’s math that goes into that. Types of stories are important. But honestly, it’s when something is ready, and when we all feel that it is great.

Zootopia 3 is in early development and was announced during the D23 Expo in August 2026, and will take the story outside of Zootopia City for the first time. While there is no official release date for the upcoming sequel, it is likely looking towards a 2029 or 2039 release window for the animated movie. Judy Hopps (voiced by Ginnifer Goodwin) and Nick Wilde (voiced by Jason Bateman) and their friend the pit vipers Gary De'Snake (voiced by Ke Huy Quan) will all be returning to the fascinating world.

The Zootopia franchise has grossed over $2.89 billion worldwide across its two theatrical releases and is known as one of Walt Disney Animation Studios' highest-earning and most successful properties. Zootopia 2 toppled records previously set by the first installment and reached nearly $2 billion at the global box office with both movies also finding a whole new appreciation on Disney+ and dominating the streaming charts consistently.

Zootopia 2 became the highest-grossing animated movie of all time in Hollywood, surpassing Pixar's Inside Out 2, and the second-highest-grossing animated movie globally, closely behind China's Ne Zha 2. With Academy Award Nominations and many awards already won, fans are eagerly anticipating the arrival of Zootopia 3.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Seyfried, who previously played the disgraced Theranos founder in 'The Dropout,' makes a surprise appearance in the A24 documentary.

Billy Evans, Elizabeth Holmes‘ husband, is finally addressing Amanda Seyfried’s portrayal of the disgraced Theranos founder in You Can See Everything.

Seyfried’s surprise appearance in the A24 documentary, directed by Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim, was the second time she’s played Holmes. She previously portrayed her in Hulu’s 2022 miniseries The Dropout.

In his first interview since You Can See Everything released last month, Evans was asked by CBS News chief correspondent Matt Gutman about his thoughts on Seyfried’s role in the film. Related Stories Movies Johnny Depp's 'Ebenezer' Unwraps $19M to Win Weekend in Box Office Return TV Billy Evans' First TV Interview to Air on 'CBS Sunday Morning': Where to Stream It Online

“I’m really glad that they included her because it shows what a difference there is between the real thing and how eager she was to play a character,” Evans replied on CBS Sunday Morning. When Gutman followed up to ask if he thought the actress played Holmes well, he added, “I’m glad Nathan was there to help her.”

“Doesn’t seem like you hold very high opinion of her acting, or of her as a person,” Gutman noted, prompting Evans to add, “Matt, I’m gonna do my best not to speak poorly about other people right now, because I’ve had a lot of people do that about me recently, and I know how it feels.”

You Can See Everything chronicles Holmes’ life in the 34 days leading up to her prison sentence, which began in May 2023. She’s now scheduled to move to a halfway house in August 2027.

Seyfried recently opened up to Rolling Stone about reprising the role, saying it was “muscle memory.” And though she wasn’t initially nervous about joining the documentary, she later got worried about the potential professional consequences of participating in the project.

“There was always this vibration of risk and that sensation of: Am I going to destroy my career in some way? Am I going to get entangled in this, like, wasp’s nest with Billy? Am I gonna say the wrong thing? Am I gonna perpetuate negativity for her? Everything was scary,” she admitted. However, she had a “strange level of trust” with the filmmakers.

Seyfried also explained why she decided to meet Evans after watching footage of his interactions with his wife and Fielder and Lance Oppenheim.

“It felt dangerous. I wanted answers. We all had this need to understand who was pulling the strings. What he was doing and how he was in the footage that I saw, how you guys were interacting — it felt yucky to me,” she explained. “I just wanted any information I could get on Billy. I became this weird Billy detective because I had gotten such strong negative feelings from him in the footage. I felt like I needed to meet this guy. It was exciting to me, and it was scary, and it was questionable.”

Seyfried previously won an Emmy for her portrayal of Holmes in The Dropout. She also plans to campaign for You Can See Everything.

Taylor Sheridan’s New Action Thriller Is Lioness Meets Sicario

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Taylor Sheridan’s new action thriller centers on a DEA black-ops mission, evoking the covert intrigue of Lioness and the cartel conflict of Sicario. The new Sheridan movie features a 1923 star as an ex-commando leading an off-the-books DEA team against CIA-protected drug dealers. The film was written by Sheridan, marking his first feature film as a screenwriter since 2021's Those Who Wish Me Dead. Along with Sheridan's new Call of Duty movie, it is one of the acclaimed writer/producer's most highly anticipated projects in the coming years.

Taylor Sheridan's upcoming action thriller F.A.S.T. combines Sicario's cartel warfare with Lioness' CIA black ops. F.A.S.T. follows a former special ops commando who leads a covert strike team to take down local drug dealers, featuring a stellar ensemble cast led by Brandon Sklenar, Sam Claflin, Jason Clarke, LaKeith Stanfield, and more. The upcoming movie is currently slated for a May 2027 release and is directed by Ben Richardson, the cinematographer of Wind River, who has also worked on previous Sheridan projects such as Yellowstone, Mayor of Kingstown, and 1883. It will be the 2-time Emmy nominee's feature film debut as a director after directing 13 episodes of 1923.

Taylor Sheridan's upcoming action thriller F.A.S.T. sounds like the ideal combination of two of his most celebrated projects, Lioness and Sicario. The military-based thriller movie stars 1923 star Brandon Sklenar as an ex-special ops officer who struggles to rebuild his life as a civilian after returning home to the United States. Sklenar's protagonist remains unnamed at this time, but it has been reported that the character will be a highly trained and experienced operative who searches for new meaning and stability outside of the armed forces. This is somewhat similar to Zoe Saldaña's main character in Lioness, Joe McNamara, who fails to balance her work and home lives cohesively.

F.A.S.T. was originally slated to be released in April 2027 but was confirmed by Warner Bros. to be officially released on May 14 of next year. Sklenar's protagonist gets a new lease on life after being recruited by the DEA to go after a dangerous group of drug traffickers who are surprisingly supported by the CIA. The core conflict, as told through the eyes of Sklenar's protagonist, exposes a theoretical conflict between the two government agencies that have very opposite agendas. This theme is consistent with both Lioness and Sicario, in which American agencies are actually at odds with each other, with top-level CIA manipulation and corruption being the underlying core of the narrative.

While F.A.S.T. has no confirmed association with Lioness or Sicario, the general concept of the upcoming Sheridan film is virtually cut from the same cloth and represents a signature characteristic within Sheridan's growing movie and television universe. Several instances in Lioness' first two seasons lean into this exact theme of covert operations, specifically the Lioness program itself, in which Joe recruits and enlists several military personnel through CIA black ops operations. While Lioness paints the CIA in a somewhat more patriotic light than F.A.S.T. seemingly intends to, it's completely on brand for Sheridan to infuse deep-seated CIA corruption as a plot framework.

Paramount+’s 3 best new series to binge-watch this week, November 13–15, 2026, feature dangerous rivalries, intense crime dramas, and fresh episodes.

As for similarities with Sicario, F.A.S.T. is even more thematically attuned to CIA manipulation, even using other agencies such as the FBI as a cover for their top-secret missions. Emily Blunt's protagonist, Kate, is an FBI agent who discovers that her task force is nothing but an instrument for a grand CIA strategy, centering on a dangerous prisoner extraction and the broader war on drugs. Given all this, Taylor Sheridan's upcoming action thriller F.A.S.T. sounds like the perfect blend of Lioness and Sicario and is poised to be another successful entry in Sheridan's filmography when it releases next Spring.

8 Long JRPGs That Are Best Played in Switch 2 Handheld Mode

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Murillo Zerbinatto is a JRPG enthusiast, writer, and editor. He has played over 300 RPGs during his gaming career, with 90% of them being JRPGs.

Many JRPGs are long by nature, whether because of seamless exploration, a narrative packed with colorful dialogue, or a post-game that increases our playtime considerably. The more adulthood knocks at the door, the less time we have to dedicate to our favorite hobby, and well, if that hobby involves playing JRPGs as mine does, you know you are in a bind.

As we approach the end of 2026, our lives are going to get even busier as the days pass, so why not add a hundred hour adventure or two to the mix?

One way to keep up with JRPGs is to buy a handheld console, which is becoming increasingly common. To ensure you have a packed catalog that gets new games every day, nothing beats having a Switch 2 at home. So, keeping portability, comfort, and time constraints in mind, I gathered some of the longest JRPGs best played in Switch 2 handheld mode.

When Final Fantasy XIV was first announced for Switch 2, I was like: okay, neat! More ways to play. As someone who has played the MMORPG for 13 years on PlayStation consoles, starting on PS3, I couldn't grasp the full potential of that announcement. Then, a friend of mine started the Free Trial, and it hit me after he shared his experience. MMORPGs are, almost by nature, a repetitive endeavor, with dailies, beast tribe quests, crafting, gathering, events, and whatnot. Doing all that in handheld mode? It is the Will of the Light.

FFXIV runs very well on Switch 2, so it's worth it even for veterans or those just starting out. Grinding was never this satisfying. The only caveat is that you have to buy the base game plus expansions again and pay a separate monthly subscription using a token made by Nintendo. But once you surpass these hurdles, portable Eorzea awaits.

I did a reverse process that left me a bit spoiled: I played Persona 5 before playing Persona 3, the portable version. That left me a bit tired of Tartarus, which felt too repetitive, even though I understood it was a product of its era. Persona 3 Reload improved the Tartarus dungeon crawling by removing random encounters and adding gimmicks like changing dungeon rules and even Shuffle Time.

Before each full moon in Persona 3, there is a bunch of downtime, just evolving Social Links, doing daily chores, and increasing your protagonist's stats. They are all a joy to watch, but since they are less heavy-loaded than the main story, you can easily enjoy them wherever you want to take your Switch 2 and just enjoy handheld mode.

I remember perfectly when I started playing Dragon Quest XI and assumed I liked the slime franchise more than Final Fantasy. After advancing a bit further, I realized I still like Final Fantasy more. Still, Dragon Quest XI is so spectacular and such a stunning evolution of the Dragon Quest formula that I fell in love immediately. DQXI mixes the simplicity and classic feel of its predecessors while modernizing and expanding all features and mechanics, making it the most ambitious title ever made in the series.

Like every Dragon Quest, the JRPG features many, many side objectives, exploration, collectibles, and of course, a robust post-game. To this day, I don't know if Act 3 is canonical or post-game, but either way, expect hundreds of hours simply to complete the main campaign and much more if you want to do everything. And there is nothing better than doing all of that on Switch 2.

Normally, I'd recommend Trails in the Sky 1st Chapter and 2nd Chapter, because they are flawless JRPGs remakes that will guarantee you hundreds of hours of playtime. However, considering I have already recommended them countless times, and in the hope that some of you heeded my call, the next step would be to go to the next arc, Crossbell, featured in the duology Trails from Zero and Trails to Azure.

Coincidentally, both recently received a Switch 2 version, adding more quality-of-life features and visual upgrades. I recognize that the whiplash of coming from a full-fledged remake like the Trails in the Sky versions to the graphical style of Zero and Azure can be a bit shocking, but hear me out: if you don't mind that and manage to finish both, reaching the ending of Trails to Azure, your mind will be blown away, trust me. Plus, there is never a wrong time to meet the members of the Special Support Section.

For when an open world feels like too much. Check out these JRPGs that don't feature an open world.

One of the biggest criticisms I read about Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave was its runtime. Players are loving Nintendo's tactical JRPG, but constantly complain that even after 100 hours, it still doesn't feel close to the end. Honestly, it seems to me that this protest is not because they are not having fun or are tired of the game, but because Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave came out in a period packed with other games, and well, you want to play it all.

But forget about that. There is no time limit on your fun, so just grab your weapons, choose your route, and enjoy your protagonist's story. Fortune's Weave introduces emblematic gameplay changes, freshening up the whole loop while still keeping elements Fire Emblem fans have learned to love and appreciate.

Honestly, I could put any Xenoblade Chronicles here, and it would fit. All of the games received, or will receive in the case of Xenoblade Chronicles 3, an enhanced version for the Nintendo Switch 2 that even added new content. And while all of them feature an immense open world you can explore from the get-go, if you want to play more leisurely in handheld mode, Xenoblade Chronicles X is the way to go.

Xenoblade Chronicles X also has plenty of story and characters to get to know, but director Tetsuya Takahashi said his goal in creating the game was to test the Wii U's technological capabilities with a gigantic world. And the madman did it. You will certainly spend much, much more time doing side content, installing probes, facing Nemeses, and flying around with your Skells than on the main story. And do you know the best way to do so? Yes, Switch 2 full-armed handheld mode.

Metaphor: ReFantazio, just like many Atlus games, is an effortlessly long JRPG. With its calendar system, every day brings a new activity, keeping the experience fresh and engaging us without us even noticing. Then, whenever I got tired of roaming around a new city, I could go hunt some monsters, tackle a new dungeon, or watch some impressive story beats unfold in this political race.

The entire design of Metaphor: ReFantazio is highly gripping, and I was completely consumed by the JRPG without even seeing the hours go by. Come to think of it, maybe suggesting playing Metaphor in handheld mode is a bit dangerous, because if I lost track of hours sitting in an uncomfortable chair, imagine if I could play it on my Switch 2 lying in bed with the air conditioning on. Okay, scratch that, too fun, too dangerous.

In its most recent fiscal report, Square Enix showed great profits and dedicated much of that success to the performance of Final Fantasy VII Rebirth on Switch 2. I understand that in terms of visual quality and performance the JRPG leaves a bit to be desired on Switch 2 compared to other platforms, but in overall experience, gameplay, and comfort, it certainly triumphs. The sprawling open world of Rebirth becomes much more practical when played in handheld mode, especially if you are hunting down minigame trophies.

What took me the longest for the Final Fantasy VII Rebirth platinum was getting high scores in a bunch of Chocobo minigames, Cactuar Crush with Aerith, and of course, Chadley's challenges. That was because I had to stay glued to my TV on my PS5, whereas if I had done it on the couch, all leisurely while watching my son play or my wife watch another K-Drama, the experience would certainly have been much more pleasant. Mind you, story-related moments and cutscenes should have your full attention, especially during the Gold Saucer!

Where do you even begin with JRPGs? These titles will provide a gentler welcome for newcomers.

Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.

‘The Good Daughter’ Author Is “Thinking About” Short Story Sequel

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After adapting her 2017 novel The Good Daughter into a Peacock series, author Karin Slaughter wants to revisit Charlie and Sam.

The writer, who also penned the streaming series and served as showrunner, revealed that she’s “actually been thinking about” following up the source novel with a short story about the sisters, who are portrayed onscreen by Meghann Fahy and Rose Byrne.

“Honestly, if they’re gonna be in a new story by Karen Slaughter, it’s never gonna be for a good thing,” she told Entertainment Weekly. “Don’t think they would be really happy in the beginning. Then, bad stuff would happen, and then they would get through it and be happy again.”

Slaughter noted that she’s already thought about where the characters are in the sequel. “Charlie [played by Fahy] and Ben [Michael Dorman] moved to Atlanta, and start jobs there and start over. I have given a lot of thought to [what’s next],” she said. Watch on Deadline

In The Good Daughter, sisters Charlotte (Fahy) and Samantha (Byrne) reunite 20 years after they survive a violent home invasion. When Charlotte witnesses a violent attack in their Georgia hometown, Samantha returns home to help her sister and their father Rusty (Brendan Gleeson) investigate the crime, both having followed him into a career of law.

Based on her own novel, Slaughter wrote all six episodes of the crime thriller, which are directed by Steph Green, now available to stream on Peacock.

Patrick Schwarzenegger Says This Small Detail Makes All The Difference In A Great Martini (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Three years with one of vodka's oldest families will apparently teach you a few things.

Patrick Schwarzenegger knows a thing or two about a good martini. After three years working with Ketel One and spending time with the family behind the vodka, the White Lotus actor has learned that some people take their cocktails very seriously.

"I'm not that severe," Schwarzenegger told me, although he's developed a few preferences of his own. His biggest? "A frosted glass. A cold glass I think makes all the difference."

I caught up with Schwarzenegger in Schiedam, Netherlands, during a Ketel One-hosted visit to the historic Nolet Distillery, where the family has been making spirits since 1691. The trip was actually his idea. After hearing so much about the distillery from the Nolet family, Schwarzenegger wanted to see everything firsthand, even getting his hands dirty by shoveling coal into the distilling equipment.

According to Schwarzenegger, family members have gotten so particular about their drinks that they'll check where a bar stores its vermouth, olives, and white wine before ordering. If certain ingredients have been sitting out rather than refrigerated, they might opt for a drier martini.

Bob Nolet sees that same attention to detail in the way people order cocktails today. "Today's martini drinkers know what they want," he told Delish, whether that's a classic dry martini, something dirty, or an espresso version.

Of course, that hasn't stopped bartenders from seeing just how far they can stretch the definition of a martini. Schwarzenegger told me he tried The Corner Store’s viral sour cream-and-onion version largely because he'd heard so much about it, something he deemed “really good.”

The Tomatini was another story. He appreciated how convincingly it captured tomato flavor, but the novelty only went so far. "I think that I could have that for a few sips, maybe one max, but then I'm tapped out of that," he said.

His more dependable order is an espresso martini, something the Nolets know particularly well. Schwarzenegger revealed that the family even arranged for espresso machines to be sent to his wedding in Idaho, giving guests a way to enjoy his favorite cocktail during the celebration.

For Carl Nolet Jr., the connection goes deeper than a shared appreciation for espresso martinis. "Patrick comes from a family that is equally committed to their craft in the entertainment space," he told Delish. "You can see that commitment through his career and how he brings his legacy into a new generation."

Carl also credits Schwarzenegger with offering a younger perspective on cocktail culture, particularly how drinking has become part of the way people socialize, host, and celebrate.

For someone who knows his way around a cocktail, Schwarzenegger doesn't actually drink all that often. Between filming, fitness, and work, he's made a rule to keep alcohol to weekends. He's also taken an interest in the growing demand for better-for-you options, pointing to canned cocktails with less sugar and real fruit juice as examples of how people's drinking habits are changing.

That curiosity extends well beyond the bar. With his involvement in the protein snack company MOSH, Schwarzenegger has become the kind of person who considers wandering supermarket aisles a perfectly good Friday night activity. One of his first stops after arriving in the Netherlands was a grocery store, and he loves comparing what's available in different places, from Los Angeles to wife Abby Champion's home state of Alabama.

His Instagram algorithm isn't helping matters, either. Food, drinks, random syrups—if an ad catches his attention, there's a decent chance it'll end up on his doorstep. "I buy everything," he admitted.

Interestingly, all that enthusiasm for trying new things doesn't necessarily translate to elaborate dinners. He and Champion love exploring restaurants together, but they're equally happy cooking at home or keeping things simple. She's a fan of Chick-fil-A and In-N-Out, while he's perfectly content with a plate of chicken and rice.

After talking martinis with Schwarzenegger and the Nolets, I might start paying a little more attention to what happens behind the bar. I'll leave the vermouth inspections to the professionals, but keeping the rest of my martini on ice until I'm ready for a fresh, frosty glass? That's a level of service I could get used to.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Johnny Depp's much-anticipated return to the big screen landed at No. 1 at the international and domestic box offices this weekend ... despite a wide divide between critics and regular moviegoers.

"Ebenezer" opened domestically to the tune of $19 million and earned an additional $23 million overseas ... according to Variety and Deadline. Not bad for a budget of $38 million!

All in all ... it seems that Scrooges aside, Johnny has done it all in one weekend, returning to screens all over the world in a triumphant way.

Garbage Wraps 2026 Tour With Mariachi-Infused Free Show at Mexico City’s Zócalo: 5 Best Moments

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Shirley Manson became the first non-Hispanic woman to conquer the iconic venue on Nov. 14.

Garbage wrapped up their 2026 tour on Saturday (Nov. 14) with a free concert at Mexico City’s Zócalo, paying tribute to a country they have visited for three decades with a nod to its culture.

The Scottish-American band performed their song “The World Is Not Enough” from the James Bond film of the same name with a mariachi arrangement. They also invited Paulina Villarreal, drummer of the Mexican rock band The Warning, to sing their iconic track “I Think I’m Paranoid.”

Garbage frontwoman Shirley Manson, 60, made history by becoming the first non-hispanic woman to headline the Zócalo, drawing a crowd of 90,000 people, according to official figures. Before her, superstars Rosalía and Shakira had packed Mexico’s most important public square in 2023 and 2026, respectively.

“It’s one of the biggest honors of our entire career [to play at the Zócalo], and the whole band is very excited,” Manson told Billboard Español before taking the stage. “Really, we’re all really emotional, and we’re so proud to be here tonight, and it’s an extraordinary… It’s a culmination of 30 years of coming to Mexico and playing in this amazing city.”

The Scottish-born artist noted that her outfit — featuring a white veil, a floral tricolor headdress and braided hair — paid homage to Mexican culture, particularly the legendary Mexican painter Frida Kahlo, whose life and work have inspired her.

During their performance, the band — also including drummer Butch Vig, guitarists Duke Erikson and Steve Marker, as well as guest bassist Nicole Fiorentino — surprised fans with their own rendition of The Cure’s classic “Lovesong.”

The setlist kicked off with “No Future,” followed by “Hold,” “Have We Met (The Void),” “Vow,” “The Men Who Rule the World,” “Chinese Fire Horse” and “Run Baby Run.” The group also performed “The Day That I Met God,” “Control,” “Fix Me Now,” “Special” and “Stupid Girl,” taking fans on a journey through 30 years of their career and eight studio albums, including Garbage (1995), Version 2.0 (1998), Beautiful Garbage (2001) and Let All That We Imagine Be the Light (2025).

Garbage joins an exclusive list of acts who have performed at Mexico’s Zócalo, the second-largest public square in the world after Tiananmen Square in Beijing. This iconic venue has hosted legendary artists and bands, such as Paul McCartney, Roger Waters, Justin Bieber, Manu Chao, Shakira, Rosalía, Café Tacvba and the late icon of Mexican regional music Vicente Fernández. The concert was organized by Mexico City’s government through its local Department of Culture.

The record for attendance at free Zócalo concerts is held by Colombian superstar Shakira, who drew over 400,000 people in March 2026, according to city authorities. She is followed by the Argentine rock band Los Fabulosos Cadillacs, who brought in 300,000 attendees in June 2023, and Grupo Firme, who gathered 280,000 fans in September 2022.

Here are the five standout moments from Garbage’s show at Mexico City’s Zócalo.

The traditional Mexican shout to express brotherhood also reached Manson, who went down into the crowd twice to make the encounter more intimate. With the Mexican flag tied around her neck, the performer sang “Chinese Fire Horse” while greeting and hugging some fans. The chant “¡Shirley, hermana, ya eres mexicana!” (“Shirley, sister, you’re already Mexican!”) echoed through the city’s iconic plaza also known as the First Square, moving her to tears.

One of the most anticipated moments of the night came with the appearance of Paulina Villarreal, a member of The Warning, whom Manson had already announced as a guest. The Mexican rocker joined the band during “I Think I’m Paranoid,” a song that has become one of Garbage’s signature tracks. The performance sealed a moment between two female rockers from different generations who have made history.

A surprise the group prepared for this special night was a heartfelt rendition of “Lovesong” by the iconic British band The Cure. The opening chords sparked euphoria among the audience, who joined Manson in singing at the top of their lungs, creating a unique and deeply moving moment.

Manson, who has stood out for her activism in favor of women’s rights and the LGBT community, took a moment during the concert to express her support for Palestine upon noticing some flags waving in the Zócalo square.

“It makes me happy that people are fighting for those who don’t have very much in life and who are in need of protection and who are struggling against a power that is threatening to completely destroy them,” the artist said. “The important thing I think to remember is that we’re all absolutely exactly the same. Doesn’t matter if you come from f—ing Palestine, whether you come from Mexico City, whether you come from Edinburgh, Scotland, or Madison, Wisconsin, or from Boston, Massachusetts. We’re all the f—ing same”.

Garbage took the iconic theme song “The World Is Not Enough,” which they originally recorded in 1999 for the James Bond film of the same name, to another level by transforming the symphonic orchestra sound into traditional Mexican music, accompanied by the Mariachi Fiesta de México. The arrangements for the piece were crafted by sound sculptor Héctor Ruiz, who elevated it to a dramatic and festive level. The performance served as the finale to a concert by a band that helped define the sound of rock in the 1990s.

Jenna Dewan Shares Her, Val Chmerkovskiy’s DWTS Plans Before Exit

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Dancing With the Stars’ Jenna Dewan shed light on her and Val Chmerkovskiy’s planned performances prior to their elimination in an exclusive interview with E! News at the 2026 Baby2Baby Gala.

Jenna Dewan and Val Chmerkovskiy were ready to take their dances a step up in the ballroom.

Nearly a week after her and her pro partner were eliminated on Dancing With the Stars, the Rookie actress shared insight into the “really great numbers” she and Chmerkovskiy’s had planned for the semi-finals and beyond.

“We were starting next week's routine, which was going to be a really beautiful foxtrot,” Dewan exclusively told E! News’ Erin Lim Rhodes at the 2026 Baby2Baby Gala presented by Paul Mitchell Nov. 14, “which I was really excited about, and a salsa.”

The Step Up star—mom to Everly, 13, with ex Channing Tatum as well as Callum, 6, and Rhiannon, 2, with fiancé Steve Kazee—added that the Latin dance “was going to be fierce and absolutely insane.”

But they weren’t going to stop there. As Dewan revealed, she and Chmerkovskiy were already also planning their freestyle routine for if they made it into the finals.

As the 45-year-old teased, the performance “was obviously going to be full out and fun.”

But Dewan shared that she’s open to finding a way for fans to get the chance to see the freestyle dance, pointing to how season 34 pairing Whitney Leavitt and Mark Ballas revealed their freestyle routine on the Call Her Daddy podcast after their elimination.

“What was beautiful about Whitney and Mark is they really paved the way,” Dewan shared. “Listen, I never say never. Maybe we can put it out there into the world in our own way you never know.

And although the mom of three is “sad” that her official time in the DWTS ballroom came to an end, she’s looking back at the experience with gratitude.

“I always keep coming back to the good of this whole journey, and I got to share it with my family, Steve and my kids, even Evie had never seen me dance live that she remembered.”

“So that experience was really beautiful to be able to end a dance and look out and see them happy and cheering for me and excited,” she continued, “and just to be in my mid-40s with my kids and be able to be like, ‘I can still do it and like there are so many good feelings that I take from it.”
